- 2026-10-17: **Phase 2a–2d: iroh 1.3, one endpoint per computer, and files over the internet road (branches `chore/iroh-1.3`, `feat/one-endpoint-per-computer`, `feat/files-on-the-link`, stacked; plan `docs/research/2026-10-16-next-phase.md`).**
  - **2a:** iroh and iroh-relay 1.3; iroh's DNS asks public resolvers never on the phone and, on a computer, only when the system lists no nameserver (`iroh_connection::dns_resolver`).
  - **2b:** a red test showed two endpoints with one key cannot both use a relay (the phone's session and control link did exactly that); dials with one device key and relays now share one endpoint, closed with the last connection.
  - **2c/2d:** a transfer with a phone linked by the internet road rides a bidirectional stream the computer opens on that link (key line, then the same resumable transfer, no TLS layer of ours: iroh proved both keys, the phone's registered at home); a request without a port asks for it. `share::Route` replaces the dial-back; the bridge's `link.rs` hands each stream to the transfer its key names. Both Settings "Files over the internet", on by default. Clipboard images take the same route (2e).
  - **Device-checked (A71 → Windows, ports closed so only iroh reached it):** a 50 MB file each way, and a 150 MB offer broken by 15 s without Wi-Fi continued, MD5s matching. Owed: checklist §85 (a relayed path, the VM, the browser's downloads, the too-old message).
- 2026-10-17: **R6e–R6f, a slow link (branch `test/slow-link`, stacked on `feat/config-dir-override`; results in the reliability spec's R6 section).** The chaos proxy can slow and lose its return path (`ChaosConfig::downlink`, `downlink_drop_rate`), and an ignored test (`server/tests/slow_link_session.rs`) runs the real server through it: at 1 Mbit/s the session held but showed 5–12 fps, the encoder kept at 6.4–8 Mbit/s since QUIC left almost no loss to see. Now the arbiter also follows what the link delivers (`DeliveryCeiling`: the transport's waits cut the rate under what got through) and steps the frame rate down (60, 30, 15; `slow_link_fps`, desktop Settings "Slow links"): the same test settles at 30 fps and about 480 kbit/s. Two fresh reviews, fixed: the ceiling sawtoothed past the link and a still desktop kept a low one (it now remembers what the link carried, climbing only under it); a cut spiralled to the floor while the old rate's frames drained (a 5-tick settle); a few seconds' dip held the ceiling low for about 15 minutes (a quiet minute forgets the remembered rate, a probe answered by a cut doubles the wait to 5 minutes, cuts average their starved ticks); a frame paced out at a lower rate was dropped (now held). Re-runs: worst 10 s window 15.0–15.6 fps after the first 30 s. The A71's reconnects were not reproduced on loopback; owed on the VM with `tc` (checklist §84).
- 2026-10-16: **The next phase begins: R6a–R6c, and a crash at server stop found and fixed (branch `feat/config-dir-override`; plan `docs/research/2026-10-16-next-phase.md`, results in the reliability spec's R6 section and checklist §84).**
  - **R6a:** `POCKETDESK_CONFIG_DIR` (with `POCKETDESK_STATE_DIR`) runs a throwaway second server beside the installed one.
  - **R6b/R6c, idle cost:** an ignored harness (`server/src/idle_cost.rs`) ranks each background loop, measured alone (their sum, 1.67 % before and 0.37 % after, over-reads the whole: separate noisy runs, each clamped at zero); the folder pollers and the mirror's rewrite each second led. One watcher (`server/src/inbox.rs`, the `notify` crate, a 5 s sweep) now wakes the consumer whose folder changed, and the mirror is written on change or every 2 s. The whole idle server: 1.35 % of one core in R6 (`main` at 0461632), 0.53 % now.
  - **The crash:** stopping the server during a session crashed about one round in 7 to 10 over several loops. A report as each fault is raised (a vectored handler, naming the module) caught it: code run inside GraphicsCapture.dll after it was unloaded, most likely a closed capture finishing on its threads. The library is pinned now: 30 rounds with no crash (at one in eight, 30 clean rounds would happen about 2 % of the time), and 10 more on the reviewed build. Whether the crashes after a reconnected session's setup were the same is not shown.
  - **Fresh review (0 Critical, 4 Important), fixed:** a media press held up to 2 s behind the report throttle, a 4 s mirror heartbeat with too little slack for the readers' 5 s rule (now 2 s), a crash handler that allocated and symbolised on the faulting thread (it could hang a dying server; now a stack buffer, raw return addresses and a log opened at start, and handled probes are left alone), and docs that claimed more than shown; plus each inbox looked at once at start, watcher errors waking all, the pin's bare-name load, and test hygiene.
- 2026-10-16: **R6, the reliability pass before ecosystem phase 2 (A71 → Windows, release build of main after phase 1; results in `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-14-phase-r-reliability.md` §R6, checklist §84).** An hour with one session start to end held (5.3 % of one core, private 82 → 87 MB); 11 Wi-Fi drops came back by themselves in 7–15 s; a server restart in 10 s; idle is 1.35 % of one core, double 2026-10-14's (the once-a-second watchers, not yet attributed). Found and fixed: the server crashed once (exit 139) as a reconnected session started, so a crash now leaves its backtrace in `<state>/crash.log` (PR #93); and the phone's control link lagged the stream by 18 to over 50 s after a reconnect, failing sends with "phone not connected" (PR #94, now dialed when the stream comes up). Also fixed: two streams on a Wi-Fi return (PR #96), and, from the Linux VM pass (1.33 % of one core in a session, Wi-Fi drops back in 10–13 s, idle 0.25 %), the example config stopping the server and VAAPI chosen with no GPU or on Windows (PR #95). Seen: a 1 Mbit/s Wi-Fi link that the 1 Mbit/s bitrate floor cannot fit; download retries that run out before a slow Wi-Fi rejoins (a resend continues, MD5 matching); files to a phone on the internet road never arriving (phase 2). **Next phase:** `docs/research/2026-10-16-next-phase.md` (R6a–R6i, phase 2 items 2a–2h, then the KDE Connect bridge 3a–3c, on by default and never a dependency).
- 2026-10-16: **Ecosystem 1g, the protocol spec and conformance vectors (branch `feat/protocol-spec`).** `docs/protocol-spec.md` is the contract for a second implementation: versions, roads, the control channel's framing, handshake and standing, how a client learns what a computer offers (the identity's `incomingCapabilities`/`outgoingCapabilities`), the stream, and conformance. `pocketdesk capabilities --packets` generates `docs/packets.md` (every control packet, its plugin and permission) from the registry the server runs, held to the build by `server/tests/packets_doc.rs`. `docs/protocol-vectors/control-v1.json` holds seven control-channel conversations that `service::vector_tests` replays against the real handler with every plugin. Found on the way: six plugins answered with packets their `outgoingCapabilities` left out (pairing, audio, monitors, privacy, windows, wake), so the identity under-stated what a computer speaks; now announced. Fresh review (0 Critical, 6 Important), fixed: the server's own pushes (endpoint, wake info, control, the HUD) and notification answers were still unannounced and the scan could not see them (it now reads all of `src/`, resolves constants and stops only at the test module; `plugins::SERVER_PUSHES`), the spec's marker timing, denial answers and permission column were wrong, and the second-identity vector could not fail (now a paired-phone test with a control).

Fresh review (0 Critical, 2 Important), fixed: a task's end was lost when its sheet closed mid-run, and a cut-short send reported none failed; plus the start before the owner's approval, a lone end, and the docs (spoofing among them). Device-checked on the A71 through the debug log: a session's start and end, a link, a file, and nothing with the switch off. Owed: checklist §83 (Tasker itself).
- 2026-10-16: **Ecosystem 1e, Home Assistant over MQTT (branch `feat/home-assistant`).** With `home_assistant = true` and `mqtt_broker` (desktop Settings "Home Assistant"), the server connects to the broker (rumqttc, plain TCP, two new crates) and announces this computer by MQTT discovery: the server's availability (its last will), sessions, a cast, an Activity event entity (each event's kind and phone, never content), each paired phone's battery, charging and connection, and a button per named task, started as a phone's start is (`start_for_home_assistant`; ask-first still asks, the prompt saying Home Assistant). Retained states sent on change, removed entities cleared, config followed within 2 s, `home_assistant.json` for the desktop's status line. SECURITY.md limit 19. Fresh review (0 Critical, 3 Important), fixed one commit each: the 2 s tick cancelled a slow connect (a `.local` broker never connected), entities removed while away stayed on the broker for good, and a retained or empty message pressed a task's button at every connect; plus guests listed, a failed read wiping the phones, `home-assistant` takeable as a phone id, the computer announced after its phones, refusals, a stale status, and the docs. Device-checked against a local broker with the A71, again after the fixes (checklist §82). Owed: Home Assistant itself.
- 2026-10-16: **Ecosystem 1d, send from the browser (branch `feat/browser-send`).** A Manifest V3 extension (`browser/`, Chromium browsers and Firefox) sends the page, a link or an image to the phone from the right-click menu through native messaging: `pocketdesk browser-host` (started by the browser with its own arguments, recognised before clap) hands a link over as `pocketdesk link` does and downloads an image (32 MB, 20 s, `http(s)`, not loopback) to send as `pocketdesk send` does. `--install` registers it (HKCU keys or the browsers' folders) for the extension's fixed id (a `key` in its manifest, held by a test); Windows `install` and desktop Settings "Send from the browser" do too. - 2026-10-16: **The ecosystem plan, and its phase 1a: events and hooks (branch `feat/events`; plan `docs/research/2026-10-16-ecosystem.md`, with the owner's ChatGPT second opinion checked against the repo).**
  - **Plan (approved 2026-10-16; macOS and iOS set aside):** phase 1 opens the edges: events and hooks (1a), an Activity Center (1b), named tasks the phone can start (1c), a browser extension, a Home Assistant bridge, Android broadcasts, the protocol spec. Then a reliability pass; phase 2 every feature on every road and computer ↔ computer; 3 KDE Connect compatibility; 4 a personal device mesh; 5 Android TV, Wear OS, tablets.
  - **How (1a):** the server appends events to owner-only `events.jsonl` (sessions with their mode, phones linking, batteries, notifications, texts, files, links; metadata only unless "details in events"); `pocketdesk events [--json]` follows it; `[hooks]` runs a command per kind through the shell with the event on stdin, 4 at once and 32 waiting, each killed at 30 s. Desktop Settings holds all three.
  - **Found on the way:** the Exec plugin runs any command a phone with Exec sends, with no deadline or output cap (to be narrowed by 1c's named tasks); and cmd's `/C` quote-stripping broke commands starting with a quoted program, in Exec and hooks (now `/S /C`).
  - **Fresh review (0 Critical, 4 Important), fixed one commit each:** a reader lost events across a rotation, a blocked rotation dropped every later event, the quoting, and bursts (config read per event, a folder as one event per file, hooks dropped at 4); plus stdin blocking the deadline, notifications only with toasts on, Settings blocking on an unknown hook, and the docs (read stdin, not `%POCKETDESK_EVENT%`).

  - **How:** "Show screen" on each saved computer; after Android's consent a `mediaProjection` service encodes the screen (H.264, long side at most 1920, about 6 Mbit/s) and sends it on a cast session (mode `2`), input only to the server and under a new per-phone permission, "Show its screen here". The server hands it to the desktop app through a loopback relay behind a token in owner-only `cast.json` (`server/src/cast_relay.rs`); the app decodes it with an `ffmpeg` sidecar into a window at the phone's shape (`desktop/src/cast_view.rs`). Gaps ask for keyframes at every hop. View only for now; control through an Accessibility service is phase 2.
  - **Found on the way:** a session's keyframe asker that waited for its channel kept the session from ever ending; ffmpeg 8 added three frames of delay (`-fflags nobuffer` drops the probed keyframe; the rawvideo encoder needs `-threads 1`).
  - **Fresh review (0 Critical, 2 Important), fixed one commit each:** ending a cast stopped whatever session the phone had opened since (and read a remote's state as its own), and the phone had no delivery backpressure, so a slow link showed an ever older picture (now `core::streaming::in_flight`, shared with the computer's video); plus the viewer after a dropped frame, the relay under slow or crowded viewers, a leaked projection and a doc comment.

Owed: checklist §77 (a stopwatch latency, the internet road, the Linux app).

- 2026-10-15: **A slow or lossy link no longer ends every session (PR #55, merged).
  - **Found on the A71:** over Wi-Fi losing about a third of its packets, every session died after 10-15 s. The desktop pushed every frame into QUIC's send buffer (megabytes) with no backpressure, QUIC shared the link among hundreds of stale frame streams, and the phone, reading streams in order, waited on one that got almost nothing; its 10 s frame watchdog then dropped the link.
  - **Fixed:** the desktop keeps at most 8 video frames undelivered (`OutStream::delivered`, quinn/noq `stopped`), so the backlog stays in the queue the transport trims to the newest keyframe; the client counts video bytes as they arrive (`read_to_end_counting`, `StreamingClient::video_bytes`) and the phone's watchdog (`StallWatch`) drops the link only when neither a frame nor a byte has come for 10 s.
  - **Fresh review (0 Critical, 2 Important), fixed:** a fixed 8-frame bound throttled far paths (a frame is delivered a round trip after it is sent), so the window is now a round trip's frames at the session's rate (8 to 64); and only the oldest frame's delivery made room, so a lost packet held up frames already delivered. Also: the bridge's counter cleared on stop. Accepted: the rest of a batch is sent while the gate waits (at most 9 frames), a blocked transport delays the encoder's IDR handling until the next batch, and the counter sees only in-order bytes.
  - **Verified:** sessions held 135 s and 90 s at about a third packet loss where each had lasted 10-15 s; loopback frame rate and latency are the same with and without the bound. Owed: checklist §55 (a healthy LAN and WAN at a real distance).

A share takes only `content://` URIs: a `file://` path would have been read with the app's own rights.
  - **Fresh review (0 Critical, 2 Important), fixed:** the extra had widened what any app could do (now behind the switch) and file paths were read with the app's rights; plus a mistyped extra taken for "none", the refusal wording, and doc gaps.
  - **Device-checked:** a share naming an unsaved address reached no computer. Owed: checklist §54 (Tasker itself, two saved computers).
  - **Found on the way:** sessions ending every ~10 s and slow transfers, first taken for the desktop's display being asleep; it was a lossy link (next bullet).

No listener: the program over the owner's own state folder (`server/src/local_api.rs`).

  - **How:** the phone sends `folder_fetch {path}`; the desktop holds it to the browser's rules and to a phone it can dial back, then offers it as the owner's folder sends are, answering first (`folder_fetch_reply`). Limits per phone: 2 of its own on the way, 10 asks a minute.
  - **Fresh review (0 Critical, 2 Important, 7 Minor), fixed one commit each:** "queued" promised to an undialable connection, the answer lost behind the offers or taken by another waiter; plus a silent denial, a reservation that could leak, a file swapped after the walk, repeated asks, the walk's bound and thread, and the answer lost when the sheet closed.
  - **Lesson:** a red-phase handler test reached the real state folder and wrote a row into the real `sent.json` (removed). Handler tests must name paths that do not exist, or use `POCKETDESK_STATE_DIR`.
  - **Device-verified (A71 → Windows):** an 8-file, three-level folder, MD5s matching, hidden files left out. Owed: checklist §52.
- 2026-10-15: **T6b, folders both ways (PR #51, merged; spec as above, T6b as-built section).**
  - **Paths:** `core::protocol::relpath` checks every name of a file's place in a folder; bad ones are refused, not repaired, and both ends check.
  - **Phone → computer:** "Send a folder to this computer" (Android's folder picker; `FolderWalk`, JVM-tested); each file is a share with `folderId`/`folder`/`path`, landing under one new `Downloads/<folder>`.
  - **Computer → phone:** the desktop app's "Choose folder…", a path or a drop; the server offers a folder file by file, with one sent-list row for it; the phone saves under one new `Download/Pocketdesk/<folder>`.
  - **Device-verified (A71 ↔ Windows):** a three-level folder each way, MD5s matching, hidden files left out, a second send in "Name (1)". Owed: checklist §51.
  - **Fresh review (2 Critical, 8 Important), fixed one commit each:** the hyphenated outbox id flattened every folder on the phone (the first device run had used a hand-made 32-hex id, which hid it; write device-test requests the way the app does); the phone kept only 16 offers; plus empty folders from refused shares, long sends split or expiring, unreported left-out names, hours of retries to a computer that went away, Windows-hidden files and the server's keys in a folder walk, unreadable folders sent as empty, and a toast per file.
  - **Re-verified:** a 20-file folder each way, from a request as the app writes it, MD5s matching, the left-out name noted.
- 2026-10-15: **T6a, transfers that resume (PR #50, merged, with an as-built section).**
  - **How:** the receiver says how much of a file it has and the SHA-256 of those bytes; the sender continues only if its own bytes hash the same, and the whole file is checked against the sender's SHA-256 before it is kept (`core::protocol::transfer`, ALPN `pocketdesk-transfer/2`). Records of unfinished files: `core::protocol::partials`.
  - **Desktop:** a share's part waits in `<state>/partials/` (Settings "Unfinished files", default 24 h); offers carry a source id and allow 4 attempts; a retry replaces the attempt still running for its file.
  - **Phone:** shares and downloads retry 3 times (5, 15, 45 s) and continue (Settings "Retry failed files"); a download's part is its hidden MediaStore row.
  - **Also fixed:** a refused share is answered (the phone waited 30 s), the phone's send has deadlines, and the desktop says "saved" only once the file is in Downloads.
  - **Fresh review (0 Critical, 4 Important), fixed:** the sweep taking a running transfer's file, large partials that could never resume, a slow final copy cut short by the retry, and refusals retried while busy computers were not.
  - **Device-verified (A71 ↔ Windows):** a 150 MB offer and a 120 MB share each broken by Wi-Fi off and continued, MD5s matching. Owed: checklist §50.
- 2026-10-15: **The Pocketdesk mark and a launch video (PR #49, merged).** `assets/brand/` (mark, icon, banner, a 21 s video made from HTML and a synthesised score); the Android launcher icon (with a themed layer) and the desktop app's icon are the mark; README opens with them.
- 2026-10-14: **Protocol audit: what Pocketdesk sends, and to whom (branch `fix/protocol-audit`; `docs/protocol.md`).**
  - **The map.** `docs/protocol.md` lists every listener, key, packet, file on disk and outside party, each row naming its source, with what is unconfirmed in its own section. SECURITY.md links it and gains two limits: the desktop names itself to any client before judging it, and permissions do not gate what the desktop pushes to a paired phone.
  - **Fixed on the way, one commit each:**
    - the pairing PIN reaches the log only when no notification can show it, and showing it no longer blocks a runtime worker;
    - the wake relay sends only onto the desktop's own networks (never loopback), needs Power (the switch now says so), and the phone reports a refusal instead of "Wake sent";
    - the control channel and transfers are TLS 1.3 only;
    - a transfer's status byte is followed by a clean close (a reset could overtake it), and a failed transfer closes at once;
    - the state folder is 0700 on Unix, best effort with a warning;
    - Android backups and device moves carry none of the app's keys or pairings;
    - `install` no longer opens UDP 1716 (nothing listens there);
    - `pocketdesk capabilities` names the real ALPNs;
    - the mDNS announcement leaves out tailnet addresses;
    - no `tailscale netcheck` on every discovery poll;
    - **the phone's relay lookups went to Cloudflare, Google and Quad9**, because iroh never had the Android context to read the network's DNS. The bridge now installs it (device-verified on the A71).
  - **Fresh review:** 0 Critical, 2 Important (a state folder that could not be chmodded stopped the server; relay refusals were silent), both fixed, plus five minors.
  - **Owed (checklist §49):** the Linux folder mode, a relay refusal on the device, backup and Smart Switch, a fresh Windows install's mDNS, and many transfer runs.
- 2026-10-14: **T2, staying connected in the background (branch `feat/background-onboarding`).**
  - **Home card:** when phone notifications or texts are on and Android may stop Pocketdesk (battery optimization, or Restricted), Home shows a card. It opens the page that lifts the limit and links the maker's guide on dontkillmyapp.com.
  - **Settings:** "Background" shows the status and has "Remind me on Home".
  - **Tests:** `BackgroundRunning` is JVM-tested; device-checked on the A71 (checklist §48).
  - **Fresh review (3 Important), fixed:**
    - the wording (optimization cuts the network while the phone sleeps; it does not stop the listener);
    - "Allow" now opens the app's own page on Android 12+;
    - Settings' status refreshes on resume.
  - **Open:** queue a notification that failed to send instead of dropping it.
- 2026-10-14: **T3 begun, getting into F-Droid (branch `feat/fdroid-ready`; plan in `docs/research/2026-10-14-fdroid.md`).**
  - **Done:**
    - The QR scanner decodes with zxing-cpp, not ML Kit. ML Kit was proprietary, sent Google usage data and failed F-Droid's scanner.
    - The APK is arm64-only, with no dependency-info block and no stray iroh cdylibs (debug APK 84 → 62 MB).
  - **The first instrumented test** (`QrDecodeTest`, run on the A71) decodes the desktop app's QR, normal and inverted. Its module grid is held to the desktop's encoder by a desktop test.
  - **Lesson:** `connectedDebugAndroidTest` uninstalls the app when it ends. The A71 lost its pairings and was paired again; its old id stays in the desktop's trusted devices. Pass `-Pandroid.injected.androidTest.leaveApksInstalledAfterRun=true`.
  - **IzzyOnDroid** rejects apps built partly with generative AI, so it is unlikely; F-Droid has no such rule yet.
  - **Next:** a pinned toolchain and one bridge build script, reproducibility, signing without re-aligning, the store listing, a tagged release, then the fdroiddata recipe.
- 2026-10-14: **T1, Check connection on the phone (branch `feat/connection-doctor`, research in `docs/research/2026-10-14-what-users-need.md`).**
  - **What it checks:**
    - the phone's network;
    - a VPN capturing home traffic (a Wi-Fi-bound retry);
    - Tailscale off for a tailnet address;
    - another subnet;
    - TCP with the error code kept;
    - a compare-only identity check (`CertManager::probe_identity`, which never saves a pin);
    - the video port, by a reserved-version QUIC packet the desktop answers statelessly;
    - battery, Data Saver and permissions.
  - **Where:** each saved computer on Home, and the session's error card. Buttons open settings, Wake or Pair again. Copy report names outcomes only.
  - **Device-checked (A71 → Windows):** all pass with the server up; "doesn't answer" with it stopped (Windows turns a stopped server into a timeout); the error card's entry.
  - **Found on the device:** the probe crashed until it installed the TLS crypto provider.
  - **Fresh review (5 Important), fixed:**
    - the key match now needs the handshake signature (a replayed certificate had passed);
    - a closed connection is not "something else";
    - "Scan code again" removes the computer and its key, then scans;
    - a non-Tailscale VPN is named;
    - no battery warning without a background feature.
- 2026-10-14: **Phase R2, long sessions and recovery (branch `fix/r2-control-link`; results in `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-14-phase-r-reliability.md`, checklist §46).**
  - **Found on the A71 against Windows:**
    - after a Wi-Fi drop the stream came back by itself, but the control connection did not, so desktop pushes stopped and every query used a throwaway connection;
    - a desktop server restart left the phone waiting for Retry.
  - **Fixed:**
    - `ControlLinkEffect` keeps the session's control connection, backing off and resting in the background;
    - `AutoReconnect` retries a link that went away, from 2 s up to 30 s, for ten minutes, with a Settings switch; an end the desktop chose waits for the person;
    - the bridge dials one control connection at a time.
  - **Re-verified:** one control reconnect after a drop; back 4 s after a server restart with no tap.
  - **Fresh review (3 Important), fixed:** retries forever after a frameless connect, no retry without a cached internet identity, and the double dial.
  - **Measured:** 35 and 30 minute sessions, with the server at 10–24 % of one core.
  - **The hour-long run (branch `fix/r2-session-leak`) found a leak:** a reconnect left the old session encoding on the desktop (up to 177 MB, double CPU), because the phone never closed a stopped stream on the wire. Now the phone closes it, and the server ends a session unheard from for 30 s. Re-verified with a monitor switch.
- 2026-10-14: **Phase R5, honest docs (branch `docs/r5-honest-docs`, stacked on `fix/r3-security`).**
  - README's "Before you start": each way a phone reaches the computer, and whether a router forward is needed (only for waking a sleeping computer from outside, or on your own relay's machine); first-contact trust; what relays and the DHT see. SECURITY.md leads with the same.
  - README's "What works where": a Linux / Windows parity table, with where each was device-checked.
  - `docs/release-checklist.md`: what comes before a tag, since the tag publishes.
  - Fixed on the way: the docs asked for Rust 1.80, but the tree needs 1.95 (egui 0.36; now `rust-version` in Cargo) and listed Tailscale as required.
- 2026-10-14: **Phase R3, hostile connections, and R4's first number (branch `fix/r3-security`; results in `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-14-phase-r-reliability.md`).**
  - **Fixed, each with a test:**
    - a phone no longer paired, or with Input, Sound or Mic turned off, loses its session within a second;
    - only the phone that took privacy mode can refresh or end it;
    - a 4 MiB cap on a control line and on a v2 frame;
    - 8 connections per address and 128 in all, counted only once a QUIC address is proven; the WAN listener holds 128;
    - device ids and v2 names must be short and printable, with no invisible characters;
    - a PIN file needs its minting time, and a PIN that paired stays spent;
    - two transfers per phone within four;
    - a folder listing fits in one packet;
    - refusals are logged at the default level.
  - **Test seam:** `POCKETDESK_STATE_DIR` (absolute only) and `use_state_dir`. The new end-to-end test drives an unpaired phone's packets through the real TLS handler.
  - **Fresh review (0 Critical, 5 Important), all fixed:** stale capability docs, forged QUIC addresses, a multi-file share losing its next file, the v2 handshake's unchecked id, and the WAN listener outside the budget.
  - **Device-verified (A71 → Windows):** a LAN session through the QUIC Retry, three back-to-back shares with matching MD5s, a 15 000-file folder listed in part with the note.
  - **R4:** the idle server uses 0.68 % of one core and 33 MB on Windows. Owed: the phone's battery, idle traffic, Linux.
  - **Accepted limits** are in SECURITY.md (5: shared connection limits; 6: privacy mode and the Power permission).
- 2026-10-14: **N7 (several phones at once), Phase R1 on Linux, and a change of plan (branch `feat/multi-phone`, with an as-built section).**
  - **Plan.** After an outside review of the repo, the user agreed to stop adding features after N7 and prove what exists first: Phase R (roadmap). N8–N11 are paused.
  - **N7.**
    - One phone drives at a time (`input_control`, `input_idle_secs`); the input task drops the others' input and releases what a phone held when control moves.
    - The desktop's Sessions tab marks the driver and can hand control over (`<state>/control/`).
    - The phones are told (`kdeconnect.pocketdesk.control`), and the session shows "Another phone is driving".
    - Kick requests are one file each (`kicks/`).
    - Fresh review: 5 Important, fixed. Two phones are owed (checklist §43).
  - **R1 on Linux.**
    - **The host:** a throwaway Azure VM (Students subscription: B1s is blocked in its regions, so B2ats v2, also free) running the release workflow's Linux build under a headless Sway, deleted after the run. Rules are in memory `azure-test-vm`.
    - **Worked over the internet from the A71:** pairing, direct QUIC, iroh WAN going direct after a second, and the clipboard both ways.
    - **Found and fixed, each re-verified on the VM:**
      - a still wlroots screen stalled the session;
      - Retry and pairing never reconnected (this was the old "waits for Retry" item);
      - a dead link was kept open;
      - input was lost for the server's life when uinput was unavailable at start;
      - the computer's name was wrong;
      - an unpaired phone kept streaming;
      - Tailscale log spam.
    - Results are in checklist §44.
  - **Local safety net:** a `.git/hooks/pre-push` (not committed) refuses pushes containing this box's real identifiers.
- 2026-10-14: **N6, wake the computer from anywhere without a second device (branch `feat/wake`, with an as-built section).** Wake had asked the sleeping computer to wake itself.
  - **Desktop:**
    - `core::local::wake` finds the adapter a phone reaches (netdev): its MAC, subnet broadcast, and whether it is wired.
    - The server pushes `kdeconnect.pocketdesk.wake_info` to paired phones; empty when `wake_announce` is off, so phones forget.
    - Settings "Wake from the phone" and "Outside wake port" (`wake_port`), and a Diagnostics "Wake" row (Windows power management, else the NDIS `*WakeOnMagicPacket` keyword; ethtool on Linux).
  - **Phone:**
    - The bridge sends the magic packet itself (`nativeWake`, three times per target) and keeps `wake_info` per computer.
    - `WakePlan` (JVM-tested) picks the targets: broadcasts at home, the public address at the outside port from away, or a relay computer.
    - Each card has "Wake settings" (method, relay, outside address and port, MAC). After Wake it probes the control port and connects ("Connect after waking"). Connect on a computer that does not answer offers "Wake and connect".
  - **Fresh review (0 Critical, 4 Important, 9 Minor), fixed:**
    - another computer's MAC could be saved;
    - awake detection missed tailnet-saved computers;
    - old relay setups regressed;
    - "Wake and connect" was missing;
    - plus the minors: no broadcast while away, validated targets, forgetting, no MAC in logs, and quoting.
  - **Verified on the A71 and this box** (a UDP listener stood in for a sleeping computer): the session taught the MAC, Wake sent the 9 expected packets, "is awake", the dialog and the offer.
  - **Owed (checklist §42):** a real wake at home and from outside through a router forward, hibernate/shutdown, Wi-Fi, a relay, and Linux.
  - **N5b merged** as PR #36.
- 2026-10-14: **N5b, texts (SMS) on the computer and replies from it (branch `feat/sms`, with Settings and as-built sections).** Built on the user's word; its device checks wait for a SIM.
  - **Phone:**
    - `SmsReceiver` forwards incoming texts.
    - The notification listener hosts the reply loop: it keeps the control connection, takes queued requests, sends with `SmsManager`, and reports the radio's real result per part.
    - Settings switches are off by default.
  - **Desktop:**
    - `core::local::sms` holds `sms.json` (owner-only) and the `sms_outbox/` the app writes.
    - The server's sms plugin and outbox watcher, and a Texts permission.
    - The desktop app's Messages tab, and new Settings: relays, the DHT, phone notifications and their limit, text notifications and history.
  - **Found on the device run, fixed:**
    - **`secure_fs::create_private_dir` on the shared state folder stripped every existing file's access entries on Windows,** so the server was locked out of the trust store. Private folders now pass their owner entry down, and the texts store never touches the state folder; the user's folder was repaired.
    - Replies were handed over twice.
  - **Fresh review (0 Critical, 7 Important), fixed in four commits:**
    - the phone now texts only for its own computer;
    - only full numbers, and at most 5 replies a minute;
    - results go to the computer that asked, with the tracking kept across restarts;
    - texts wait on the phone while the computer is away;
    - text notifications share the flood guard;
    - `sms.json` is written atomically, and a bad read never wipes it;
    - an unconfirmed reply is handed over again, at most 3 times.
  - **Re-verified on the A71 after the fixes:** handed over once, and failed with "no SIM chosen for texts".
  - **Found on the re-run:** control links could go half-open (the desktop's side reset, the phone's still open), and the phone never dialed again. TCP keepalive is now on at both ends. Bridge panics and failed reply ticks now reach logcat.
  - **User preference (2026-10-14):** every feature's knobs go in Settings on the phone and the desktop.
- 2026-10-14: **N5c part 1, a phone notification closed on the phone closes on the computer (branch `feat/notification-dismissal`).**
  - **How:**
    - The phone sends `kdeconnect.notification {id, isCancel}` for a notification the computer was shown, and for no other.
    - The server knows each one by a phone-scoped tag (16 hex digits of SHA-256 of phone id and notification id).
    - Windows shows tagged toasts in a "phone" group and removes them from the Action Center (`RemoveGroupedTagWithId`).
    - Linux shows phone notifications over D-Bus first, keeping their ids, and closes them with `CloseNotification`.
  - **Verified on the A71 and this box:** a toast leaves the Action Center when its notification is swiped away on the phone.
  - **Not done:**
    - desktop → phone dismissal and action buttons: the Windows listener needs a package identity, and toast buttons need activation;
    - N5b SMS waits for a phone with a SIM.
- 2026-10-14: **N5a, the phone's notifications on the computer (branch `feat/phone-notifications`, with an as-built section).** N5 is split three ways: N5a this, N5b SMS, N5c actions and dismissal both ways.
  - **New:**
    - A `NotificationListenerService` sends the phone's notifications to the most recently used paired computer.
    - Off by default. Settings "This phone's notifications" has the switch, which opens Android's access page, and a per-app opt-in list.
    - `NotificationForwardRule` (JVM-tested) applies the lock-screen privacy, the one-time-code filter, and the never-send cases.
    - On the desktop, a new per-device permission, Notifications, gates the packet, and at most 20 a minute are shown.
    - README's old claim ("receive Android notifications on your Linux desktop") had nothing behind it; it is now true.
  - **Fresh review (1 Critical, 6 Important), all fixed:** the Critical was the desktop relay echoing the server's own notifications, a phone's and the pairing PIN's, to every paired phone.
  - **Verified on the A71 and this box:** private shown as "New notification", flood 31 → 20, opt-in, the desktop permission.
  - **Owed (checklist §39):** a real chat app, a messaging app that hides its content, battery when the computer is out of reach, and Linux.
- 2026-10-14: **N4, image clipboard both ways (branch `feat/image-clipboard`, with an as-built section).**
  - **New:**
    - When the desktop's clipboard holds an image and no text, its reply to the phone's 4 s poll carries `{image: {id, size}}`, the id being the PNG's SHA-256. The phone fetches it with `clipboard_image_fetch`, and sends its own with `clipboard_image`.
    - Either way the desktop dials back, and the PNG travels inside the pinned transfer TLS, at most 32 MiB and 40 megapixels. Both packet types are under the Clipboard permission, not Files.
    - Windows reads the registered "PNG" format, else `CF_DIB` converted (`platform::clip_image`, tested on every OS), and writes both. Linux uses `wl-paste` / `wl-copy` with `image/png`.
    - The phone puts a received image on its clipboard through a FileProvider. `ImageClipRule` (JVM-tested) keeps an image from bouncing back.
  - **Verified on the A71 and this box:** desktop to phone, once each, including the same image again after text in between.
  - **Owed (checklist §38):** phone to desktop on a device (the A71 has no app that copies an image), pasting, the permission switch, a 4K screenshot, and Linux.
  - **Fresh review (0 Critical, 6 Important), all fixed:** a decode bomb, the clipboard held open during encoding, stale phone sync state, an old image sent at session start, real photos dropped or sideways, and failed fetches.
- 2026-10-14: **N3, finding the desktop on the Mainline DHT (branch `feat/dht-lookup`, stacked on `feat/own-relay`, with an as-built section).**
  - **New:**
    - The desktop publishes a record signed with its iroh key on the BitTorrent Mainline DHT (`iroh-mainline-address-lookup`). `dht_publish` says what it holds: `relay` (the default), `addresses` (also public IPs), or `off`. LAN, tailnet and documentation addresses, and relays known by a private address or a home-network name, are never published.
    - The server joins the DHT in the background, so the WAN endpoint never waits on it, and reads its own record back. `wan_status.json` carries `dht`/`dht_found`, and Diagnostics' new "Found by id" row reports them.
    - The endpoint packet says `dht: true` when there is a record. The phone's WAN dial then adds a resolve-only lookup, merged by iroh with the cached address, and drops it once connected. There is no Kotlin change.
    - `core::streaming::dht` resolves the DHT's bootstrap names off the runtime (n0-mainline does it with blocking DNS and never retries) and binds a port the OS picks, not 6881.
  - **Already true, now written down:** IPv6 was bound on both ends all along. Our `bind_addr(0.0.0.0:0)` replaces only iroh's IPv4 default.
  - **Verified on the A71 and this box:** a cached identity made stale (wrong n0 relay, no addresses, LAN port closed) found the desktop on the DHT in about 3 s and went direct. With `dht: false` it timed out.
  - **Fresh review (0 Critical, 7 Important), fixed one commit each:** the bootstrap and port, the phone's node living all session, verdicts stuck for 15 minutes, Diagnostics over-claiming, LAN names in the record, `dht: true` for an empty record, and docs understating what the DHT sees.
  - **Owed (checklist §37):** a session from outside, `addresses` with a real public IPv6, a UDP-blocking network, and the Linux host.
- 2026-10-13: **N2, your own relay (branch `feat/own-relay`).**
  - **New:**
    - `pocketdesk relay` runs iroh's relay server: plain on a port (default 3340), or HTTPS on 80/443 with Let's Encrypt (`--hostname`).
    - It admits only listed endpoints. `--allow-paired` admits this desktop and its phones' registered WAN keys, re-read per connection. `--open` admits anyone. With neither, it refuses to start.
    - `pocketdesk relay-ids` prints the ids to `--allow` on a relay elsewhere.
    - The desktop's `relay_urls` config points its WAN endpoint at your relays. `endpoint.json` announces them, and the phone learns them on its next home connection.
    - Diagnostics names the relay in use and says "not measured" for "From outside": a plain relay has no QUIC address discovery, so iroh cannot measure UDP or the NAT through it.
  - **Fixed on the device run:** pairing again with the same certificate dropped the phone's WAN key, so the next WAN session was refused.
  - **Verified on the A71 and this box:** a WAN session rode the relay on the desktop (`wan_relayed`). An unlisted endpoint is refused (integration test).
  - **Fresh review (0 Critical, 3 Important, plus one Minor raised), fixed one commit each:**
    - the relay admitted WAN keys of devices no longer paired (expired guests, unpaired phones);
    - the phone still homed on n0's relays: it now dials with only the desktop's own relays when it announces only those (device-verified: the phone's home relay became the user's relay);
    - a wrong `relay_urls` entry failed silently: entries must be `http(s)://` with a host, and Diagnostics says when one is refused or the relay is not reached;
    - the Let's Encrypt cache's private keys were not owner-only.

    Ten minors are deferred in the spec's as-built section.
  - **Owed (checklist §36):** a session from outside the home network, HTTPS mode with a real name, and the relay refusing an unpaired phone on the device.
- 2026-10-13: **Linux Link is Pocketdesk everywhere (branch `chore/rename-pocketdesk`).**
  - **New names:**
    - the crates `pocketdesk-core`, `pocketdesk-server` and `pocketdesk-android-bridge`;
    - the server command `pocketdesk`, with `pd` as a short name (the same program);
    - the desktop app's exe, now `pocketdesk-app`;
    - the config and state folders `pocketdesk`, migrated from `linux-link` on first use by `core::local::paths`, which the server's own paths now go through;
    - the wire protocol (ALPNs, `POCKETDESK_HELLO`/`OK`, `kdeconnect.pocketdesk.*`, `_pocketdesk._tcp`), the `POCKETDESK_*` env vars, the uinput names, the systemd unit, AUR, man and CI;
    - the Android app `dev.pocketdesk.android`, a new install that must pair again.
  - **Kept on purpose:**
    - the Windows installer's legacy cleanup (it still removes the old "Linux Link" task and firewall group);
    - dated specs, plans and this journal, as records.
  - **The server is matched by its exact names** (`pocketdesk`, `pd`), not a prefix, since `pocketdesk-app` and test runners now share the prefix.
  - **Verified on the A71 and this box:**
    - the folders moved with the pairings intact;
    - `pd --version` works;
    - the new app found the computer over mDNS, paired by PIN, and streamed.
  - **Found on the way:** after pairing from the refusal card, the app waited for Retry instead of reconnecting by itself (fixed 2026-10-14, see the N7/R1 bullet).
- 2026-10-13: **N1, reaching the desktop from outside without Tailscale, step 1 (branch `feat/wan-insight`).**
  - **Already there, now confirmed:**
    - the built-in iroh WAN path (hole punching, n0's free relays as the fallback, both ends held to their keys);
    - UPnP / NAT-PMP / PCP port mapping, which is iroh's default and stays on under the `Minimal` preset;
    - the phone's "WAN · direct / relayed" badge.
  - **New:**
    - The server records iroh's network report in `wan_status.json`: yes/no facts only (UDP, the IPv4 NAT's mapping variance, public IPv6, captive portal), never an address. It removes the file, and `endpoint.json`, at start.
    - Diagnostics turns those facts into "From outside" (this computer's NAT allows direct connections, or may need the relay) and "IPv6".
  - **Verified on this box:** friendly NAT, no public IPv6.
  - **Owed (checklist §35):** the cellular test. The phone has no mobile service, so it needs a SIM or a hotspot.
  - **Review:** 3 Important, fixed (a stale status from an earlier run, over-claiming wording, an IPv6-polluted NAT verdict).
- 2026-10-13: **F10, desktop diagnostics (branch `feat/diagnostics`).**
  - **The tab.** The desktop app's new Diagnostics tab checks:
    - the server;
    - the control port (it answers) and the stream port (it is held);
    - the firewall on Windows: the enabled inbound rules, both `install`'s Pocketdesk group and program rules Windows added for linux-link.exe;
    - the addresses phones can use;
    - the internet path (`endpoint.json`).

    It shows the last session (outcome, encoder, e2e, encode and RTT p50/p99) and copies a debug report (no PINs, keys or file names; device ids cut to 8 characters).
  - **The encoder.** Session records now name it (`encoder`, the last one if a fallback happened).
  - **Code.** The checks are in `core::local::diagnostics`, tested on loopback.
  - **Fresh review (4 Important), fixed:**
    - a starting server is given 30 s before its ports are judged or probed;
    - the firewall check counts only the rules that apply to the active profile and the running exe, and Block rules;
    - the internet path is ready only once a relay is reached;
    - the PowerShell query has a 10 s deadline.
  - **Verified on this box** (an ignored test that prints the report): during startup the port checks wait; once running, all six are ok. Owed (checklist §34): the Linux host, a Public network without rules, a Block rule.
  - **Next (agreed with the user):** the roadmap's N1–N11, starting with cross-network without Tailscale (N1–N3).
- 2026-10-13: **F11, shortcuts outside the app (branch `feat/shortcuts`).**
  - **Launcher shortcuts.** "Connect to …" for each saved computer, most recent first, then "Wake …" for those with a Wake-on-LAN address, four at most. They refresh when the app stops, and a connect is reported for launcher ranking.
  - **Quick Settings tile and a home-screen widget.** Both connect to the last computer, or open the app when none is saved.
  - **One path for all three.** Each opens `MainActivity` with `ACTION_CONNECT` or `ACTION_WAKE` and an address, which opens only a computer already saved. The intent can come from any app, so it never adds one. A launch from Recents replays nothing.
  - **Tests.** `ShortcutPlan` holds the choices, under JVM tests.
  - **Device-verified (A71):** the shortcuts listed; a connect shortcut opening a LAN session; an unsaved address opening nothing; the tile connecting; the widget provider registered.
  - **Owed (checklist §33):** placing the widget, a Wake shortcut, pinned shortcuts, and Android ≤13's tile path.
- 2026-10-13: **Phase C of mutual auth: file transfers inside TLS (branch `feat/transfer-tls`, with an as-built section).**
  - **Roles.** The phone still listens and the desktop still dials, but each transfer opens with TLS 1.3 (ALPN `linux-link-transfer/1`), and each end accepts exactly one certificate:
    - the phone, the desktop it pinned for the address it asked (an IP or a saved name);
    - the desktop, the key bound to the phone that asked. A paired phone with no key bound yet moves no files.

    The one-time key, the size and the status byte run inside, flushed.
  - **Fresh review (0 Critical, 2 Important), fixed one commit each:**
    - a computer saved by name had lost transfers (pins are now looked up by the control channel's label, and Remove forgets named pins too);
    - the docs.

    Minors fixed: the bound-key requirement, the desktop's certificate read once, ALPN checked both ends, the phone checking incoming connections side by side (silent ones had starved the desktop's), and the pin looked up after the request.
  - **Device-verified (A71 ↔ Windows, LAN):** desktop to phone (an offer) 3 MB and phone to desktop (a share) 2 MB, MD5s matching.
  - **Found on the way, not phase C:** a share whose content URI the app cannot read crashes `ShareActivity` (a `SecurityException` from `startForegroundService`); fixed separately.
- 2026-10-13: **Phase B of mutual auth: the control channel inside TLS (branch `feat/control-tls`, with an as-built section).**
  - **Wire.** TCP 1716 opens with TLS 1.3 (ALPN `linux-link-control/1`), with the stream's certificates.
    - The desktop requires the phone's certificate.
    - The phone checks the desktop against the same per-address pin, so a QR pin covers pairing from the first byte.
    - A plain client gets `LINUX_LINK_ERR 1 tls-required`.
  - **Identity.**
    - The phone's identity is read in the handshake, and the connection goes by that device id, not its IP.
    - Every packet re-reads its standing: paired with its key, unpaired, or a paired id with another key, which may only pair again.
    - Pairing binds the key that paired. The owner's PIN replaces it, which recovers a phone whose certificate changed. A guest never takes a paired id.
  - **Fresh review (1 Critical, 4 Important), all fixed, one commit each:**
    - a v2 identity could re-point a TCP connection at a paired phone (the id maps are gone);
    - trust was decided once, at admission;
    - re-pairing could not recover a changed key;
    - the phone's pin file could lose or guess pins;
    - the phone's first identity could race.

    Minors: PIN pacing, bounded handshake lines, a quiet disconnect log, and pushes only to the right desktop.
  - **Device-verified on the A71 (LAN):** plain refused; TLS control and video; a tampered key held to pairing only; Pair again with the owner's PIN recovering it; the WAN key re-registered.
  - **Still open:** without the QR code, first contact is trust on first use. File transfers moved inside TLS in phase C (next bullet up).
- 2026-10-13: **Device keys, a phone proves who it is (branch `feat/device-keys`, on top of the verifier fix in PR #21).**
  - **Verifier fix (#21).** The phone's TLS verifier had accepted every handshake signature, so a pinned desktop certificate proved nothing: anyone could present a copy. It now verifies signatures with the ring provider.
  - **Phase A of mutual auth.**
    - The phone presents its persisted certificate as a TLS client certificate on LAN QUIC.
    - The server offers client auth and verifies the signature.
    - `Connection::peer_key()` gives the proven key (cert SHA-256, or iroh endpoint id).
    - The session gate gets a `SessionClaim` and checks `<state>/device_keys.json`.
  - **Binding rules:**
    - First use binds only a device with no key (existing pairings migrate), and only a LAN TLS key.
    - Mismatch, or no key where one is bound, is refused, logged and notified.
    - The WAN key never binds by first use. It is registered only from a LAN session its TLS key proved (`[0xFC,0x00]` stream); a WAN session from a device with no keys passes by id, as before.
    - The check only reads. Keys bind after permissions and the owner's approval allow the session, and of two racing first sessions the second is refused.
    - The phone keeps one iroh key, written atomically.
    - v2 sessions are checked read-only.
    - Pairing with the owner's PIN (never a guest grant), unpair and the app's Remove clear keys.
    - The store takes the trust store's lock file. An unreadable store refuses everyone with its own notification, and Remove or re-pairing sets it aside.
  - **Fresh review (1 Critical, 3 Important), all fixed, one commit each:** WAN first-use binding (lockout, or an internet squatter binding first), binding before the gate (a denied impostor kept its key), a guest PIN clearing the owner phone's keys, no recovery or lock on the store.
  - **Device-verified on the A71, again after the review fixes:** a keyless WAN session binds nothing; the first LAN session binds the certificate and registers the iroh key; WAN then matches; a tampered stored key is refused (logged, the phone's refusal card); restoring recovers. An iroh session over a direct LAN path shows the phone's LAN address: read the gate's `Device keys decided` debug line, not the address.
  - **Still open (phase B):** the TCP control channel (clipboard, files, notifications, pairing) still trusts the announced id and is unencrypted.
- 2026-10-13: **Repo review fixes (branch `chore/repo-review`) and the screen-switch crash (branch `fix/monitor-switch`, PR #19).**
  - **License.** The root `LICENSE` was garbled text, not a license. It is now `LICENSE-MIT` plus `LICENSE-APACHE` (canonical), matching `MIT OR Apache-2.0`. Every crate declares the license, and the README has a License section.
  - **Rename.** Finished where people read it: CONTRIBUTING, the issue templates, the config example, the dev setup, and the Android theme. `plan.md` is marked historical. Kept on purpose, and documented: the `linux-link` command, its paths, the wire protocol, and the package id `dev.linuxlink.android` (changing it would break updates and pairings).
  - **Security.** `SECURITY.md` describes each connection, what protects it, and the known limits, and GitHub private vulnerability reporting is now enabled.
    - **Top open security item:** a paired phone is identified by the `deviceId` it announces in plain text, not by a key it proves, so anyone on the same LAN who has seen that id can claim it. The fix is mutual authentication, binding the id to a key.
    - The control channel and file transfers are not encrypted.
  - **Releases.** `release.yml` publishes on a `vX.Y.Z` tag, or a pre-release on `vX.Y.Z-beta.N`, after checking the tag against the Cargo and Android versions.
    - **Contents:** the Linux tar.gz (server, desktop app, man page), the Windows zip, and the Android APK (release-signed when signing secrets exist, else the debug build), with `SHA256SUMS` and release notes.
    - **Dry run:** running it by hand publishes nothing.
    - `install.sh` follows the new names. `linux-link --version` exists now (install.sh relied on it).
    - Version 0.2.0 everywhere. No release has been published yet.
  - **Screen switch crash (device-found, `IllegalStateException: Invalid to call at Released state`).** A connect in flight when a switch stopped the stream installed its session afterwards, and its stopped decoder restarted on a dead surface. Fixed in two places:
    - bridge connect generations, with the monitor part of the session's identity;
    - a `DecodeGate` that cannot reopen, and codec errors that end the stream, not the app.

    Also added: a screen-switcher chip per monitor in the session chrome (F9). Verified on the A71 over LAN and WAN, including switches during a slow connect.
  - **CI fix (PR #18).** A race between two bridge tests over the shared offer queue failed #17's Linux job; they now share a test lock.
- 2026-10-13: **F7b, files both ways and the remote browser (branch `feat/file-transfer-b`, stacked on `feat/file-transfer`, plan `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-10-13-file-transfer-b.md`).**
  - **One mechanism.** The phone asks with `kdeconnect.linuxlink.file_fetch` `{path | offer, port, key}`, and the desktop dials only the transport's peer, as in F7a. On the socket go `key\n`, then the size as a big-endian u64, then the bytes. A refusal comes back as `file_fetch_refused` `{key, reason}`.
  - **Status byte, both ways.** The receiver ends every transfer with one status byte (1 saved, 0 failed), F7a's share included, so "sent" means saved.
  - **Browse.** Replies carry the folder's path, its parent and each entry's own path, so the phone can open folders; that was impossible before. Hidden entries, the server's state and config folders, and Windows device names are refused, by listing and by path.
  - **Desktop to phone.**
    - The app's Files tab writes `outbox/<id>.json`. You name the file with a phone picker, a path field, "Choose file…" (Windows) or a drop on the window.
    - The server pushes `file_offer` `{id, name, size}` to a phone in a session. An offer is single use, lasts 10 minutes and is for its own phone.
    - The phone fetches it into MediaStore `Downloads/Pocketdesk`, pending until complete.
    - `sent.json` always reaches an end: an expired offer is swept, and the phone can decline with `file_offer_declined`.
  - **Phone.** "Files on this computer" in Quick Settings, and incoming offers downloaded during the session. The bridge's `download.rs` adds `nativeFetchFile`, `nativeTakeFileOffers` and `nativeDeclineOffer`.
  - **Device-verified (A71 ↔ Windows, LAN):**
    - browse home → sub-folder → Up, with hidden entries gone;
    - a 3 MB download and a 5 MB desktop send, MD5s matching;
    - a phone share waiting for the save;
    - the Files tab and "Choose file…", the second copy saved as `name (1).bin`;
    - "failed: phone not connected" for a send to a phone that has left.
  - **Owed (checklist §31):** drag and drop, opening a saved file from the notification, full disk, more than 1 GB, Android 8–9, and the Linux host.
  - **Review (fresh reviewer, 0 Critical, 4 Important), all fixed:**
    - a download by path reached hidden files and the server's identity key;
    - sends stuck at "offered";
    - an offer pushed to a share's unread connection;
    - plus five minors.
  - **The fifth Important is a ruling.** An offer is bound only as far as the in-band device id, which is the F5 limit.
  - **Also fixed, found on the device run:** the pairing sheet could not take a PIN after "Show PIN on desktop".
  - **Desktop-safety lesson.** uiautomator still lists faded session chrome, so "chrome visible" checks are unreliable: two stray taps opened the keyboard-layout flyout. For device tests, turn the phone's Input permission off at the server; the session is then forced view-only. Restore it afterwards.
  - Gates: fmt, workspace clippy `-D warnings`, `cargo test` (core lib, server lib 160, desktop 32), bridge 36, Kotlin tests, and lint at or under the 21 baseline. Linux gates are owed (CI).
- 2026-10-13: **File transfer made safe, then F7a, phone to desktop (branches `fix/share-safety`, merged as PR #15, and `feat/file-transfer`).**
  - **Never worked, and was unsafe.** Phone-to-desktop file sharing had never completed, because both ends listened and neither dialed.
    - The server joined the phone's file name onto Downloads, so any paired device could write anywhere.
    - It listened on 0.0.0.0 at a port the phone chose.
    - The file browser could be coerced into NTLM authentication via UNC paths.
  - **Fixes (PR #15, two security reviews):**
    - safe names, no overwrite, a declared size of at most 8 GiB, and `.part` files with a drop guard;
    - the desktop dials the transport's peer (`DeviceSender::peer_addr`), never loopback or link-local;
    - a one-time transfer key authenticates the connection;
    - at most two transfers at once, each with a deadline;
    - Mark of the Web on Windows, and lexical-then-canonical browse paths.
  - **F7a:**
    - **Phone:** `SEND_MULTIPLE`; a `dataSync` foreground service that queues shares, streams from the provider's file descriptor (no cache copy) and shows a progress notification.
    - **Desktop:** a notification per received file, `received.json` (the last 50), and the app's Files tab with Show in folder.
  - **Device-verified (A71 → Windows, LAN):** 0.3, 1.5, 2, 4 and 6 MB files with matching MD5s; no overwrite; `ZoneId=3`; three queued shares; the final notification.
  - **Limits:** LAN only (not `adb reverse`, the v2 channel or WAN). Desktop-to-phone and the remote browser are F7b.
- 2026-10-13: **F6, computers on this network (branch `feat/lan-discovery`).**
  - **Server.** The mDNS announcement (`_linux-link._tcp`) carries the device id and stream port in TXT. A new `lan_discovery` config key (on by default, "Visible on this network" in the desktop app) can turn it off. The announcement is withdrawn with an mDNS goodbye on a graceful stop.
  - **Phone.** Home browses with NsdManager while in front. Unsaved computers are listed under "On this network"; saved ones get a label, matched by address or paired id. The LAN IPv4 address is preferred, and a computer is listed only if its control port answers (mdns-sd's 75-minute cache otherwise shows ghosts).
  - **Review (fresh reviewer, 5 Important):**
    - A spoofed announcement could collect the PIN. A found computer now needs a confirmation that points to the QR scan, and it is saved only once paired.
    - A moved computer was hidden by id matching; matching is now by address only.
    - Only one address was probed, with no retry. Now up to four candidates are probed, with retries.
    - The server announced before its listener was up.
    - **Android 17 blocks all LAN traffic for SDK-37 apps without `ACCESS_LOCAL_NETWORK`.** It is now requested at launch. This affected the whole app, not just F6, and is untested on Android 17.
  - **Device-verified (A71 ↔ Windows, same Wi-Fi):**
    - found in about 0.2 s;
    - a test announcer listed, then removed live on its goodbye;
    - a killed server's cached record not listed;
    - a decoy first address skipped;
    - the confirmation shown, Connect opening a session with the PIN sheet and saving nothing.
  - Owed (checklist §29): pairing from a found computer, Android ≤13 and 17, the Linux host, and the desktop switch end to end.
- 2026-10-13: **F5, per-device permissions and session approval (branch `feat/approvals-permissions`, with an as-built section).**
  - **Permissions.** Each paired device has switches for input, clipboard, files, audio, mic, power and exec, stored in `trusted_devices.json`. Exec is off unless granted. One `capability_for(packet_type)` table gates control packets on TCP and v2. The stream gate forces view-only, skips audio and drops mic per device. The desktop app's Devices tab sets them.
  - **Approval.** "Ask before each session" holds a stream at the gate for up to 30 s. The request goes in `pending_approvals.json` with a toast, and the app comes to the front with Allow once / Always allow / Deny, answering through `approvals/<id>.json`. No answer is a deny.
  - **Phone.** The first frame gets 45 s, and a hint appears while the session is held. A connection the desktop closes now reaches the UI within about 1 s with its reason (`Connection::close_reason`, a new JNI export), instead of the frame-gap watchdog.
  - **Device-verified (Galaxy A71 → Windows, WAN):** hold → Allow once → video; hold → Deny → refusal card in 2 s. Answers were written to the file, not clicked. Owed (checklist §28): the app's prompt clicks, timeout, each switch's effect on the device, and a LAN prompt.
  - **Review (fresh reviewer, 6 Important):** five fixed, one commit each. A notification reply needed no clipboard permission. The control gate failed open on an unreadable store. Trust-store writers could lose each other's change and shared one temp file; every writer now goes through `TrustStore::update` under a lock file. A running session outlived a narrowing change or removal; the app now kicks it. The stream gate was skipped with pairing off. Minors fixed: future-stamped answers, the sweep deleting an in-flight answer, one answer for all of a phone's held requests, and expired grants' permissions.
  - **Limits:** device identity is the in-band deviceId (IP-keyed on TCP), so a paired device can claim another's id; this predates F5, and binding the id to the pinned cert is the fix. A denied control packet is dropped without a reply. Approval holds the stream only, not the control channel.
- 2026-10-13: **Trackpad gestures (roadmap F3, branch `feat/trackpad-gestures`) and the keyboard's review fix pass.**
  - **F3.** Both trackpads (trackpad mode and the foldable dock) feed one pure, tested `TrackpadGesture` engine and share one sender:
    - one-, two- and three-finger taps are left, right and middle clicks;
    - tap-then-drag holds the left button, and a second tap is a double click;
    - two fingers scroll;
    - cursor speed is a Settings slider (0.5×–3×) with sub-pixel carry.

    On the A71 → Windows, tap-and-drag selected a Notepad line, and the slider persists. Multi-finger taps are unit-tested only (adb cannot make them; checklist §27).
  - **F3 review fixes (fresh review: 4 Important).**
    - Tap-then-drag had reached the desktop as a double click plus a drag (title bars maximised, files opened). A one-finger tap is now held for the 300 ms drag window, and nothing is pressed while it waits.
    - A drag torn down mid-gesture is released from a scope that outlives the view; the server has no release-all (2272). Direct touch gets the same fix.
    - Two-finger scroll waits for the slop, so a right-click no longer scrolls first. It is now natural, at about a notch per 24 dp; it had been one notch per pixel, and its direction was mislabelled.
    - Pinch-zoom in trackpad mode is still open.
  - **Keyboard fix pass (fresh review: 1 Critical, 7 Important).**
    - Re-composing after any reset duplicated text. The shadow's offset now starts over with the IME, and a region it cannot take resyncs.
    - The shadow resyncs whenever the caret may have moved: arrows and other bar keys, shortcuts, a selection away from the end, a touch on the desktop.
    - IME key events keep it in step.
    - A latch is one key.
    - Modifiers latch on the first tap.
    - The IME does not learn passwords.
    - The sink takes focus only while open.
    - `hotkey` releases what it held on a failed hold.
    - Landscape gets a one-row bar.

    PR #9 merged with CI green on every job.
  - **Desktop-safety rule for device tests:** adb taps during a session reach the real desktop. Sessions end with force-stop, and typing tests go only into a scratch Notepad, only while the user is away. Three stray clicks happened on 2026-10-12 before this rule.
  - Gates: 74 Kotlin tests (31 keyboard, 9 trackpad), lint at 21.
- 2026-10-12: **Feature roadmap and the in-session keyboard (branch `feat/in-session-keyboard`; research `docs/research/2026-10-12-competitor-features.md`, roadmap `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-12-feature-roadmap.md`, spec `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-12-in-session-keyboard-design.md`).**
  - **Roadmap.** A competitor survey (RustDesk, Moonlight/Apollo, Parsec, Jump, Chrome and Microsoft RD, KDE Connect, scrcpy, Phone Link) ranks eleven features for the phone and desktop apps:
    - F1 keyboard;
    - F2 send-text;
    - F3 trackpad gestures;
    - F4 Linux text path;
    - F5 approvals and permissions;
    - F6 discovery;
    - F7 file transfer;
    - F8 image clipboard;
    - F9 session polish;
    - F10 diagnostics;
    - F11 launcher shortcuts.
  - **F1, the keyboard.** The phone's IME types into an invisible sink view through a shadow model of the composing text, so typing is live and swipe or autocorrect rewrites become backspaces plus text. Modifiers latch on the phone only and go out as press-tap-release combos, so none can stick on the desktop. An accessory bar holds the modifiers, Esc/Tab/arrows, Home/End/PgUp/PgDn/Del/Ins, F1–F12 and Ctrl+Alt+Del. A keyboard chip opens it, and Back closes it. The phone↔server key contract grew to 93 codes (Home/MOVE_HOME, Insert, Backslash).
  - **Verified on the Galaxy A71 → Windows** (Notepad read back through UI Automation): typing, autocorrect, the hardware-key path, Ctrl-latch shortcuts, Backspace, Home/Del/End, Back to close. Owed in checklist §26: swipe and dictation, locks, F-keys, a hardware keyboard, landscape, the Linux host, and non-English keyboards.
  - **The server segfault reproduced (exit 139).** It came right after codec negotiation, on a LAN session, after about an hour of uptime that included a relay outage. The server now runs under gdb (`thread apply all bt` on crash) for every device session. No backtrace yet: the next run under gdb did not crash.
  - Gates: 23 new keyboard JVM tests (Kotlin 57 total), lint at 21, bridge clippy + 23 tests, core and server key-contract tests.
- 2026-10-12: **The phone plays the desktop's sound (branch `feat/phone-audio`, cut from `feat/pocketdesk`, plan `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-10-12-phone-audio-playback.md`).**
  - A MediaCodec Opus decoder feeds a low-latency AudioTrack, with a fixed 60/150 ms budget, audio focus and a live "Desktop sound on this phone" switch (on by default). It follows the video stream's lifetime. The decisions it makes (late/restart sequences, prebuffer, cap hysteresis, starvation refill, the 32-bit head wrap) are in `PlayoutPolicy` under JVM tests; `OpusHead` builds the csd the bare packets need.
  - Fixed on the way: the bridge's frame and audio receives waited while holding `STREAMING_HANDLE`, which every input send locks first, so a still screen delayed every tap by up to 50 ms. Both receivers now have their own locks (`VIDEO_RX`, `AUDIO_RX`), and audio packets carry their sequence to Kotlin.
  - `linux-link capabilities` now says `desktop -> phone ... played: yes`. `docs/capabilities.md` was edited by hand to match and owes a Linux regeneration.
  - **Final review (fresh reviewer) + first device run (Galaxy A71, USB + WAN direct):** the plan had the player starting only *after* the stream, because `H264Decoder.start()` is the decode loop. Both the device and the review caught it. Fixed by a generation-guarded `AudioLifecycle` (stream up before the loop, down in a `finally`). Also fixed: the empty-poll spin, and focus taken at connect or never regained (`FocusPolicy`: lazy request, delayed gain, a permanent loss left alone). On the device the player now starts with the session, a low-latency 48 kHz stereo AudioTrack reaches `state:started`, and the playout thread averages about 4.5 % of a core.
  - Not verified: by ear (quality, lag, switch, calls/other apps, PiP — checklist §25). Lazy focus follows packets, and Windows loopback sends silent packets while any app holds a stream. Out of scope, as roadmap items: measured jitter sizing, drift, A/V sync, PLC (2792-2795). Ten review minors are deferred.
  - Gates: fmt; workspace and `client` clippy `-D warnings`; `cargo test` (core lib 279, server lib 116, desktop 22, 0 failed); bridge clippy + 23 tests; arm64 `.so`; `assembleDebug` + `lintDebug` at 21 findings with none new; 34 Kotlin tests.
- 2026-09-22: **Phase-C UI/UX polish pass landed (plan: `~/.qoder/plans/tidal-inlet-warbler.md`; C1–C9, checklist §21) — then substantially fixed on first device run, see the next bullet.** The app got a real design system: `ui/theme/` tokens + `Prefs`-driven `LinuxLinkTheme` (system-follow light/dark, Material You on 31+, brand pine/amber fallback; themes.xml Light + new values-night kills the old dark-window mismatch), edge-to-edge everywhere and an **immersive auto-hide session** (system bars hidden in `RemoteScreen` only; one `chromeVisible` timer fades/reveals HUD + bottom row + action disc + shortcut bar together, sheets suspend it). Error UX is humanized (`ui/UiError.kt` maps raw Rust strings → friendly resources; raw goes to Logcat) with one snackbar host replacing 13 toasts and a centered connect/fail card with Retry + Re-pair. **No Material-icons-extended in the offline cache → `ui/Icons.kt` is a Canvas-glyph set** (`LlGlyph` value class of stable `DrawScope` primitives — superseded the same day by vector XML, see the device-remediation bullet below). Sheets/pickers became real `ListItem`s with radio/switch selected states (the "· current" suffixes are gone), pairing got progress + autofocus + reveal-PIN eye + animated success check, Home got an empty-state explainer + a new **Settings screen** (theme/dynamic-color/keep-screen-on/reveal-PIN/haptics/language/about) behind `AnimatedContent` routes in `MainActivity`, the siren now shows a full-screen Silence dialog, clipboard Clear confirms, `ui/Haptics.kt` (`View.performHapticFeedback`, Prefs-gated; `HapticFeedbackConstants.TOGGLE` resolved reflectively because the API-37 jar predates it) ticks the disc/mode/picks/chips, the shortcut bar regrouped to System|Workspaces chips, and the session notification ships real vector icons (`res/drawable/ic_stat_linux_link|ic_notif_disconnect|ic_notif_lock.xml`) + a channel description. Kotlin-only — no Rust/JNI/wire change; locale strings now **161/161/161** en/es/ta.
- 2026-09-22: **Phase-C remediated on-device (OPPO CPH2359, tailnet) — the first build the user actually ran, and it looked broken.** The headline bug was the icon layer: the runtime-Canvas `LlGlyph` set rendered as a tiny dot in the corner of its box on device, so the session action disc was a blank square and every sheet row lost its affordance. `ui/Icons.kt` now wraps 28 hand-authored `res/drawable/ic_ll_*.xml` Material vectors (24x24, black fill) drawn through `Image` + `painterResource` + `ColorFilter.tint` — the same framework path the notification icons already proved works here, and the `LlIcon` call sites are unchanged. Three more fixes only a real screen surfaced: `StatsHud` became a `FlowRow` (a six-item `Row` is wider than a portrait phone, which squashed the trailing metrics into a one-character-per-line column), session chrome became inset-aware and self-describing (light mode chip with its mode glyph, scrimmed circular Exit, bordered HUD panels, a dark floating `ShortcutBar` pill instead of a surfaceVariant sheet that vanished over video), and **any** touch on the stream now summons the faded chrome via `PointerEventPass.Initial` — the old design had only invisible 30 dp edge strips, which made the menu practically unreachable after the 6 s fade, and observing on the Initial pass means the summon tap still reaches the desktop instead of being swallowed. The app also shipped with no launcher icon (the launcher drew its default blob): `mipmap-anydpi/ic_launcher*.xml` is an adaptive icon on the brand pine plate, and its foreground had to be **filled** paths inside a scaled group — ColorOS refuses to rasterize stroke-only adaptive foregrounds (it substitutes a placeholder) and zooms the layer past the 108 dp safe zone. Verified live on device: session chrome, QuickSettings sheet, pickers, Home, Settings (theme radios apply and persist), Exit, and the launcher icon. Strings stay **161/161/161** en/es/ta (the `Mode:` prefix dropped from the chip label). Lint 19 → 18 findings (`MissingApplicationIcon` cleared, none added). Kotlin-only — no Rust/JNI/wire change.
- 2026-09-22: **Dead video link no longer looks like a healthy session.** A desktop-side teardown (server kick, sleep, Wi-Fi loss) left the phone showing the last frame under normal chrome with no way out: the bridge clears its `STREAMING_ACTIVE` flag only on a *client-initiated* `stop_streaming`, so nothing ever reported the drop and the HUD kept quoting a lifetime-average fps (the frame counter and start time are reset by that same explicit stop, and by nothing else). Fixed in Kotlin where the truth is visible — `H264Decoder`'s pull loop now treats a 10 s frame gap as a dead link (`keyint=60` guarantees packets far faster than that, so it cannot trip on a still desktop) and calls back through `RemoteDesktopView` onto the existing error card; Retry was verified end to end (kick → card in 10 s → Retry → new QUIC session in the server log → 25 s of live HUD with no false positive). The card also gained the warning glyph + centred copy the design called for. Device-confirmed alongside: the session notification ships the real `ic_stat_linux_link` monitor icon and iconed Disconnect / Lock-desktop actions with a channel description. **Open, NOT mine and actively being worked elsewhere:** the server journal shows the phone's v1 TCP control connection flapping (baseline ~4 reconnects/min, 18-36/min while the session sheets are used, each dying in ~20 ms on a `Broken pipe` in the clipboard/battery plugin), and the server's own `Kicked 1 stale session(s) due to reconnect storm` fires on it — the installed bridge `.so` is a mid-edit build of the uncommitted `core/src/protocol/{backoff,connection,v2}.rs` work, so re-check this against a clean tree before treating it as a shipping regression. Kotlin-only again — no Rust/JNI/wire change. **Superseded 2026-09-24:** the flapping is root-caused to the Android bridge opening a throwaway control connection per query, not to the in-flight `core/src/protocol/*` work — see the control-connection bullet below.
- 2026-10-09: **Windows host W0 + W1 landed on `feat/windows-host` (spec `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-08-windows-host-design.md`, plan `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-10-09-windows-host.md`).** The server now builds, lints and tests on Windows (`x86_64-pc-windows-gnu`): W0 split every Linux backend behind target-specific deps and cfg (capture → `capture_linux.rs`, PipeWire audio → `audio_capture_pipewire.rs`, privacy → `privacy/{linux,unsupported}.rs`, Hyprland sockets behind one helper, uinput gated, an evdev key-name shim in `core::input::evdev_codes` held to the evdev crate by a Linux test). W1 added the Windows backends: Windows.Graphics.Capture with a 1 s idle heartbeat + DXGI duplication fallback (`CaptureBackend::{Wgc,Dxgi}`), AMF/Quick Sync/Media Foundation encoder rungs runtime-verified like NVENC (Auto: NVENC → AMF → QSV → MF → x264, AUD/filler NALs filtered so the per-NAL packetizer sees NVENC's shape), SendInput with set-1 scancodes / UTF-16 text / virtual-desktop absolute moves, per-monitor-v2 DPI awareness, Win32 clipboard, WinRT toasts, `if-addrs` local IPs, `COMPUTERNAME`, owner-only DACLs via `core::secure_fs` (which also fixed Linux writing `identity.key` 0644), a graceful `linux-link stop` over a named event (hardened after a security review: no squatted event, no PATH-resolved `taskkill`, no killing a reused pid) plus SIGTERM handling on Linux, and `linux-link install/uninstall` (per-user logon task + firewall rules) with `start --background` file logs. The sidecar's stdout/stderr moved from `O_NONBLOCK` to reader threads on **every** OS. **Box-verified** with ignored live tests on this PC (RTX 3060 + Radeon iGPU): WGC + DXGI real frames, NVENC/AMF/MF 60-frame encodes opening on SPS with an IDR, SendInput pointer landing on the computed pixel + a held key, clipboard Unicode round-trip, toast accepted. **Not yet run:** the phone end-to-end (checklist §23), `install`, `stop`; and no Linux gate had run on these commits (since run in CI on PR #7; see the W2–W4 bullet). Windows gates green: fmt, clippy `-D warnings`, `cargo test` (core lib 232 + 12 ignored, server lib 105 + 4 ignored, 0 failed).
- 2026-10-12: **Pocketdesk: user-facing rename, a desktop app, and QR pairing (branch `feat/pocketdesk`, cut from `feat/windows-host`, plan `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-10-12-pocketdesk-desktop-app.md`).**
  - **Rename.** Everything a person sees now says Pocketdesk: app strings (en/es/ta), toasts, CLI help, the Windows task and firewall group (install/uninstall also remove the legacy "Linux Link" ones), "Pocketdesk Mic", README and packaging. The GitHub repo is now `sreevarshan-xenoz/Pocketdesk`; old URLs redirect. Kept on purpose:
    - the `linux-link` binary and its config/state paths;
    - the `dev.linuxlink.android` package;
    - the `kdeconnect.linuxlink.*` packets and ALPNs;
    - the toast app id;
    - the uinput device names (the Linux privacy grab matches the "Linux Link" prefix).
  - **`core::local`** (feature `local`) holds the files the server and the app share:
    - paths;
    - `Config`, now savable through `toml_edit` (keeps comments and unknown keys; an unchanged save writes nothing);
