    - the PIN file (OS-RNG mint, owner-only, 300 s TTL, far-future stamps refused);
    - the live-session mirror with a 5 s freshness rule;
    - kick requests;
    - pid-plus-image liveness;
    - the certificate fingerprint;
    - `endpoint.json`, which the server now keeps current;
    - the `pocketdesk://pair` link and synchronous local IPs.

    The trust store now writes temp-then-rename.
  - **Desktop app** (`desktop/`, binary `pocketdesk`, eframe 0.36 + tray-icon):
    - Status with Start/Restart; Restart runs `linux-link stop` and then starts the way the server is installed;
    - Pair: a QR plus the PIN in digits with a countdown, verified to decode; one code pairs one phone;
    - Devices with Remove; Sessions with End; Settings; Logs;
    - close-to-tray and single instance.

    It has its own unit tests and was checked against the live server on this box.
  - **Phone.**
    - A `pocketdesk://pair` deep link and a CameraX 1.6 + ML Kit (bundled) scanner, both feeding one pairing flow.
    - The flow pins the code's certificate fingerprint before it contacts each address, then pairs, then saves the host and endpoint and opens the session.
    - The app's first JVM unit tests (13) cover it, plus a shared fixture URI that holds the Rust encoder and the Kotlin parser together.
  - **Security review (three rounds) — fixed, and claims corrected:**
    - Windows PINs were derived from the clock: there is no `/dev/urandom`, and the fallback used nanoseconds. Now OS RNG.
    - The PIN file was world-readable on Linux.
    - A link could silently override trust. Now the phone always confirms first, leading with the addresses, the name shown only as "calls itself" and sanitized, plus a warning when the code was not scanned.
    - A link may never replace a different pin for an address. Removing a computer forgets its pin.
    - A pin added by an attempt that fails to pair is taken back.
    - Honest limit: the fingerprint protects the QUIC stream only; the PIN rides the control channel, which is plain TCP.
  - **Final branch review (2 Critical, 6 Important) — fixed, one commit each:**
    - The Linux build of the app: eframe lacked its x11/wayland features. Linux now ships without a tray (closing quits) instead of panicking on GTK.
    - A non-ASCII fingerprint could crash the app through the JNI pin call.
    - A PIN was reusable and unlimited for its 5 minutes. Now it pairs one phone, and the server discards it after 5 wrong guesses. The Pair tab never re-mints on its own after a discard (that would hand a guesser fresh tries), and it withdraws its code when left, hidden or quit.
    - The scanner now releases the camera; a pairing link is offered once, not again on recreation.
    - `tailscale ip` has a 2 s limit and opens no console window, and runs off the app's UI thread.
    - The 16 Minor findings are deferred in the ledger.
  - **Verified on device (Galaxy A71, USB):**
    - a code from the app → opened on the phone → confirm → pinned → paired → streaming;
    - a forged link is refused or rolled back.
  - **Not verified:**
    - a camera scan of a real code;
    - LAN pairing without `adb reverse`;
    - the tray menu itself;
    - Start/Restart (never run on this box);
    - the Linux build of the app (it runs there without a tray);
    - the fix pass's phone behavior: camera release, the link not re-offered, the one-code UI (checklist §24; no phone was attached).
  - **Open:** one server segfault (exit 139, no WER record), right after codec negotiation on a WAN session. It did not reproduce in 5 more sessions under gdb, and its cause is unknown.
  - Windows gates green: workspace clippy `-D warnings` incl. `desktop`, `cargo test` (core lib 279, server lib 116, desktop 22, 0 failed), bridge clippy + 20 tests, `assembleDebug` + `lintDebug` at 21 findings, 13 Kotlin tests.
- 2026-10-12: **Windows host W2–W4 landed and the first phone run happened (Galaxy A71, Android 16, over USB) — the run found that video had never rendered from a sidecar encoder.**
  - **Landed:**
    - W2 audio: WASAPI loopback into the shared Opus path; endpoint volume/mute/switch via `IPolicyConfig`; the mic relay into a virtual-cable endpoint (`mic_relay_device`).
    - W3 plugins: battery, power mapping, exec through `System32\cmd.exe`, siren, window list, a polled Workspace HUD.
    - W4 privacy: low-level hooks with a TTL watchdog, and a capture-excluded ring.
    - W5, partly: a notification listener that needs a package identity, the checklist §23 run, the plan ticked honestly. Still open: the CI windows job, the release zip, the optional sparse MSIX.
  - **Defects the phone found, all fixed on the branch, each its own commit:**
    - The sidecar wrote a raw elementary stream that the packetizer split into one NAL per packet, so SPS, PPS and the IDR slice shipped apart. It also could not end a frame until the next one began. FFmpeg now writes FLV, `flv_demux` turns each tag into one Annex-B access unit, and `encode_frame` returns every ready packet. NVENC with `-delay 0 -zerolatency 1` answers each 720p frame in ~4 ms with no frames held; AMF holds 1 and MF 16, which the encoder learns and stops waiting on.
    - `RustCore.receiveFrames` dropped the last frame of every batch, which includes every session's opening keyframe — since the bridge port.
    - A decoder reconfigure re-fed only the keyframe and broke every P-frame after it.
    - Whole-monitor taps were normalized over the frame but injected over the two-monitor layout; they are now remapped in the streamer, on Windows only.
    - The loss controller judged pre-stream iroh probing as congestion and rebuilt the encoder 4× at every session start.
    - Registering a short query connection evicted the phone's control connection, losing HUD pushes for the session.
    - The bridge's tracing never reached logcat: a stdout subscriber won the global slot.
  - **Device-verified:** pairing, NVENC + WGC video at e2e 13–19 ms, pixel-accurate taps with two monitors, clipboard both ways in Unicode, HUD window titles and battery.
  - **Not verified:** keyboard (the app has no in-session keyboard — backlog), LAN QUIC (`adb reverse` is TCP-only, so video rode iroh), `install`/`stop`, power/lock (never run on this box).
  - **Linux gates: run in CI on PR #7 (2026-10-12)** for the whole stack (Windows host + Pocketdesk + phone audio). On ubuntu: fmt, workspace clippy `-D warnings`, build, and `cargo test` (core lib 284, server lib 95 + 1 failed); the four core profiles; and a `windows` job, green after a host-identity test fix. The one failure is `a_faulted_uinput_device_is_rebuilt_and_the_packet_retried`, which also fails on `main` (the runner has no `/dev/uinput`). Still unrun anywhere: the Linux NVENC/VAAPI sidecar rungs with FLV output on real hardware (`encoder.rs`). CI also fails `android` at `setup-android` (the retired `tools` package) and `audit` (the same 6 advisories as `main`, incl. quinn-proto RUSTSEC-2026-0185 and rustls RUSTSEC-2026-0285), both pre-existing.
  - Windows gates green: fmt, clippy `-D warnings`, `cargo test` (core lib 249, server lib 115, 0 failed); bridge clippy + 16 tests; `assembleDebug` + `lintDebug` with no new finding in a changed file.
- 2026-09-24: **Hyprland output events now drive something (`210f240`; roadmap 2066 CLOSED, hardware-unverified).** `monitoradded`/`monitorremoved` were read from socket2, matched no whitelist arm and were dropped, so the Workspace HUD's `screen` box and every window-crop geometry could stay wrong for up to the 4 s snapshot tick after a display came or went. Both names now trigger an immediate `state_packet` re-push to every client through `hypr_events::push_state_to_clients` (the tick's inline fan-out was folded into the same helper rather than duplicated). They stay out of the forwarded *event* stream on purpose: `WorkspaceHud.applyEvent` ends in `else -> this`, so an output delta would be a packet the phone agrees to handle but cannot. Two claims corrected while doing this — the audit's "the phone keeps a monitor list that no longer exists" was wrong (the monitors plugin answers `enumerate_monitors()` live per query and the picker refetches on open), and the `monitorchanged` name a KDE-Connect mental model expects is not emitted by this Hyprland at all: `strings $(which Hyprland)` shows exactly two monitor events, so mode/scale/orientation changes cannot be event-driven and stay 2566, with the capture-side restart as 2567. Unplug/replug has never been observed here — one built-in output, and Hyprland's socket1 write dispatchers are broken upstream so no fake hotplug exists; checklist §6 carries the log check. Gates: fmt, workspace clippy `-D warnings`, `cargo test --workspace` (server lib 94, 0 failed) incl. the new classification test.
- 2026-09-24: **The `us`-layout assumption is now measured, not implicit (`fc1b368`; roadmap 2065 PARTIAL).** Every code the uinput path emits is a *physical position* chosen against a `us` keyboard, so on any other layout the phone's `a` injects `q` — and nothing in the tree had ever looked at what layout the desktop had. `InputInjector::new_uinput` now probes `XKB_DEFAULT_LAYOUT`, falls back to `localectl status`'s `X11 Layout:` line, and reports at construction: `debug!` on `us`, `WARN` naming the layout when it is not, `WARN` about the assumption when neither probe answers. It deliberately does **not** reject the session, because the phone's ordinary key events are position-based too — refusing a non-`us` desktop takes the keyboard away from every non-US user instead of fixing them; the real fix is 2665, whose repro the roadmap now carries (`XKB_DEFAULT_LAYOUT=fr`, tap `a`, desktop receives `q` while the journal names the layout). Both report branches were observed live on this box (which reports `us`); the comma-group/`us(intl)` classification and the `localectl` parser are unit-tested against that tool's real output.
- 2026-09-24: **Input *state* correctness: the drag presses, the gamepad releases, a faulted uinput device rebuilds (`60ae055`, `bcbec8e`, `3db2f5d`; roadmap 2061-2064).** Three defects with one shape — a packet that carries state was handled as a one-shot command. Direct touch sent absolute motion and a release but never a press, so a "drag" was a warp and the only click came from a helper nothing called; the gesture loop now warps, presses, moves and releases, and a two-finger cancel lifts the button it put down. The gamepad packet is a button-mask snapshot, so every mapped bit re-pressed on each packet and nothing was ever released — one DPad tap left an arrow key held for the rest of the session; the handler diffs against the previous mask and emits edges, and a test asserts the release set equals the press set for all seven mapped bits (still no Android source, 2695, so unit-verified only). And both uinput devices were built at startup and never revisited, so one `write` fault ended input for the whole session; emits now go through a single helper that rebuilds the device, retries once and logs which device and action faulted — proven by a test that `dup2`s a live device's descriptor onto a read-only `/dev/null`, so the next write fails `EBADF` exactly as a destroyed device's would, then asserts both devices recover (self-skips where `/dev/uinput` is absent; `libc` added as a dev-dependency for the `dup2`). Residuals named, not hidden: a release packet lost mid-drag still leaves the button held because no release-all exists (2272), and the rebuild cannot rescue the packet that faulted, because libinput opens a freshly created device asynchronously. Gates: fmt, `clippy --workspace --all-targets -D warnings`, `cargo test --workspace` (server lib 89, 0 failed).
- 2026-09-24: **2057-2060 + 2271 closed — the keyboard stopped degrading silently, and both ends are now held to one contract (`295ff1c`, `bf88325`, `a9b903d`, `db75497`, `dc9fc22`).** The root defect was translation loss: the uinput path took the evdev code the phone sent, converted it to an enigo `Key` and back, so any code the table didn't name came out as a control character (KEY_A → U+001E, KEY_SCROLLLOCK → 'F') instead of failing. Now `InputInjector::key()` emits the received code directly on uinput (the keyboard device declares 0..255) and the enigo round-trip remains only on the X11 rung — which is why that rung's table had to become complete: `KEYCODE_MAP` went 26 → ~110 rows built from `KeyCode::KEY_*.0` rather than magic numbers, covering both Shifts/Controls/Alts/Supers (right-Alt and right-Super as `Key::Other(0xFFEA/0xFFEC)`, because enigo 0.6.1 has no Linux variant for them), F1-F24, locks, nav, insert/delete, print-screen, volume and media, the menu key, and digits/letters/punctuation as `Key::Unicode`; `char_to_keycode` is derived from that same table instead of duplicating it. The client side had its own real bug: extending F-keys by contiguous range would have made the phone's F11 a **NumLock** press and F12 an **F1** press (evdev 69/70, not 87/88). The contract is `core::input::keys::PHONE_EVDEV_KEY_RANGES` — 91 codes, `core::input` ungated so neither end imports the other's table — with the bridge asserting its map emits *exactly* that set and the server asserting it can *name* every member, so a new key on one side breaks the other's test. Gates: fmt, workspace clippy, core `client`/`client,wan`/`wan`/`encode` profiles, `cargo test --workspace` (0 failed), bridge clippy + 14 tests, server 21 input_injector tests, arm64 `.so` rebuild, `assembleDebug` + `lintDebug` at the 18-finding baseline. **Honest limits:** this box injects through uinput, so the completed enigo table is unit-verified only until checklist §3's second-backend keyboard run happens on a real X11/XWayland session; 2663 is PARTIAL (one *set* is shared, the two *tables* are still hand-written); shifted text on uinput is still broken (2666 — `text()` emits no Shift, so pasted capitals arrive lowercase); the input-state follow-ups 2061-2064 closed the same day are their own bullet below.
- 2026-09-24: **2056 closed — the phone's HUD now says what each number was measured over (`42edf12`, `e7ac404`).** `fps`/`kbps` were the session's totals divided by its uptime, which answers a question nobody asks while watching a picture: a link that stalled a minute ago still read as healthy, a link that just recovered still read as stalled, and `rtt` was whichever one-second poll the 500 ms display happened to land on. New `android/bridge/src/rates.rs` keeps the counter reads the display has made (10 s of them), diffs them across the newest **3 s**, and discards the whole history when a counter goes backwards — a rebuilt session counts from 0, so averaging across that boundary would subtract one computer's traffic from another's; `RTT_HISTORY` replaces the single `STREAMING_RTT_US` atomic with the session's last 8 polls, `get_streaming_rtt()` returns their **median**, and `STREAMING_START_TIME` is gone because nothing could use it any more. The DTO carries the basis (`rate_window_ms`, `rtt_samples`), and `StatsHud` prints it in a caption (`rates over 2.6s · rtt median of 3`) while dimming fps/kbps until the window is full and `rtt` until its first sample. **Confidence here is a stated sample count, not an interval, and that is the ceiling:** quinn 0.11's public RTT surface is one smoothed `Connection::rtt()`, iroh 1.3's per-path `PathStats` carries one smoothed `rtt`, and both stacks name a variance only inside their qlog-only `RecoveryMetrics` — a type no application reads. Gates: fmt, bridge clippy `-D warnings`, workspace clippy, `cargo test -p linux-link-android-bridge` (14, incl. 8 new window/median/reset unit tests), `cargo test --workspace` (all green), arm64 `.so` rebuild, `assembleDebug`, `lintDebug` at the 18-finding baseline with no finding in either changed Kotlin file. Device behavior unverified — checklist §2d is the new section (fill-in after connect, the stall check that the lifetime average used to fail, and the basis reset on reconnect).
- 2026-09-24: **2054 closed — and "advertised audio the phone cannot play" was not a doc problem, it was a video stall.** Three commits: the client's single demux loop now *offers* each audio packet (`try_deliver_audio`: skip when the 8-slot queue is full, log once when the consumer is gone, never `await`, never `break` the loop), because the Android app has no Opus playout path and so never drained it — 160 ms into any session with audio, video queued behind sound nobody hears; the desktop no longer synthesizes silence at 50 packets/s when PipeWire capture is unavailable, since that was what made the stall the normal case instead of a corner one (no capture → log the reason, end the audio task; `_pw_session`'s Drop cancels the *shared* pipeline token, so the early exit is only reachable on the branch that never had a session); and `core::capabilities::AudioFormat` gained `played_on_the_receiving_end`, so `linux-link capabilities`, its JSON and the generated `docs/capabilities.md` state that `desktop -> phone` is encoded and sent but heard by no one while `phone -> desktop` really does reach a `pw-loopback` sink — with a unit test that fails if the table starts claiming otherwise, and `server/tests/capabilities_doc.rs` holding the doc to the build. Roadmap 2791-2800 (a real phone-side player) is what would turn that `no` into a `yes`; the README's audio row needed no change because it only ever claimed *control* (volume/mute/routing), and the app's sheet is that control panel. Gates: fmt, workspace + `client` + `client,wan` + `capture,encode` clippy, `cargo test --workspace` 341 passed / 0 failed (core lib 227 + 11 ignored, server lib 83).
- 2026-09-24: **Phase 2 opened: the two headline "reported number that is not a measurement" defects are closed (`12167ce`, `ac9b5b9`, `5f45934`).** The session HUD's `frame_drops` was a literal `0`; it is now the holes in the server's per-frame sequence numbers as the client's receive loop sees them — the only vantage that can know a frame never arrived whatever dropped it — measured as `highest - lowest + 1 - arrived` so an out-of-order stream completion corrects itself instead of accumulating phantom drops, with the window restarted on sequence 0 because a rebuilt encoder counts from 0 again (**2051**, and **2052**'s "delete it otherwise" is moot). Packet loss now moves the encoder's rate: `LossCeiling` folds into the live bitrate arbiter as `configured.min(relay_cap).min(preset_ceil).min(loss_cap)`, reading the transport's own cumulative `lost_packets`/`datagrams_sent` each 2 s tick — 20 % off per congested tick down to a 1 Mbit/s floor, 10 % back up per clean tick until the term is inert, with a 100-datagram sample floor so a still desktop cannot floor a healthy link (**2053**). Two things this surfaced that are worth knowing before touching bitrate code: the arbiter in `streamer.rs` is the *only* rate owner (the checklist's LAN/preset/loss items are the observable proof of every term in it), and the module's RTT controller was **dead** — `with_adaptive_bitrate` had no callers, so `AdaptiveBitrate`/`BitrateProfiles`/`AdaptiveBitrateMonitor` never saw a connection and the README's "RTT-based congestion control" described a no-op on top of unreachable code; it is deleted rather than revived, and 2821 (loss + RTT in one place) is now work against the arbiter. Loss response is unit-tested only — proving it needs a link that actually drops (checklist §11).
- 2026-09-24: **Phase 1 step 3 closed — one session record now carries all five percentile tails, measured by whoever can see them (`7a1623e`, `57094da`, `dba5d9e`, `ff1b526`, `6113600`).** The design decision was to ship **raw duration batches** from the device and let the *server* fold them through the same `core::metrics::Samples` reservoir that builds `enc_*`/`rtt_*`, instead of computing client-side percentiles that would not be comparable with the desktop's: `dec_*`, `rnd_*` and `e2e_*` in a line are the same code as `enc_*`, so tails across the whole chain can be read against each other. Two new `InputPacket` tags carry them (13 `LinkFeedback` = the phone's own rtt + received-side loss, 14 `ClientSamples` = kind + count + `u32` µs, exact-length checked; tags are never renumbered, unknown tags still hard-fail decode, and the two ends ship together with no compat shim). On the phone, `H264Decoder.kt` pairs each released output buffer with the feed that produced it — valid only because the stream is B-frame-free (R2#1) — clears the pairing on every codec open, MIME swap and resolution reconfigure rather than reporting a guess, caps a render gap at 1 s (longer is an idle desktop, not a slow panel), and bounds the pending-feed queue. Timing source is `elapsedRealtimeNanos/1000`, **not** `elapsedRealtimeMicros`: this SDK's android.jar does not expose the latter, the same trimming that already took `MediaCodecInfo.isDecoder`. Teardown of the bridge's sample path is non-blocking (a per-frame JNI push into a bounded batch, drained by the existing 1 s RTT tick), and `report_samples`/`SampleBatch::{new,clear}` are public so the phone's decoder is a first-class measurement source rather than a special case. Also fixed on the way: the server's connection monitor had a `data.len() == 16` pre-filter that silently swallowed any 16-byte tagged frame — dispatch is by tag again, which means an *older* client's untagged 16-byte stats buffer now logs one parse warning per second instead of being eaten unseen. `server/tests/session_record.rs` is the Phase-1 gate artifact: a real capture→encode→send→receive session over loopback QUIC that asserts the emitted record holds `rtt_*`/`enc_*`/`dec_*`/`rnd_*`/`e2e_*`, the link block and `phone_rtt`/`phone_lost` — so the client→server half of the chain is proven on this host and only the "a phone actually measures it" remainder stays device-owed. It is `#[ignore]`d (needs a desktop) and it caught two behaviors worth knowing: a session shorter than the server's 5 s path sampler emits no `rtt_*`, no link block and therefore no `phone_*` either (the client's readings hang off that block), and closing the QUIC connection underneath a running `StreamingClient::start()` does **not** end it — the cancel token does, which is the shape of the frozen-frame bug the phone's frame-gap watchdog exists for. Gates: fmt, workspace + `client`/`client,wan`/`wan`/`encode` clippy, bridge clippy `-D warnings`, `cargo test --workspace` (core 221 lib, 0 failed) and `--features wan`, arm64 `.so` with `nativeRecordSample` verified in it, `assembleDebug`, `lintDebug` at the 18-finding baseline with no finding in either changed Kotlin file. Docs (README sessions row, `man/linux-link.1`, checklist §Transport, plan step 3 + exit gate) now name which tail is measured where.
- 2026-09-24: **Phase 1 measurement half landed: the project now has tails, and a pinned way to regress them.** Before this there were zero occurrences of percentile/p95/histogram anywhere in the tree, so the class of bug this project keeps shipping — a change that hurts 5 % of frames — was structurally invisible. `core::metrics::Samples` is a bounded Algorithm-R reservoir (capacity 8192) emitting whole-millisecond `Summary{p50,p90,p95,p99,max}`, and `core/src/streaming/bench.rs` pins the workload it is measured against: 720p frames generated from `(width, height, index)` — a static diagonal gradient plus a moving 128×128 block with position-dependent pixels, so motion estimation gets a real residual — because storing 300 BGRA frames would be 1.1 GiB of git objects that anyone could quietly soften. `linux-link bench` (server `bench.rs`) turns that into a CI-readable contract: exit 0 pass / 1 regression / 2 usage / 3 **not comparable** / 4 unmeasured, where 3 is a first-class outcome — encode throughput does not travel between CPUs, and a baseline that disagrees on schema, backend, geometry, rate, preset, codec, frames, warmup or host is refused rather than judged. Two baselines are committed from this box (software p50 7 / p99 9; in-process VAAPI p50 4 / p99 6, actually on the iHD `renderD129`), and `--repeat` exists because the first recordings were contaminated at load average 6-8 (p50 11-18 ms) — best-of-7 recovered the quiet number, so the rule is keep-the-fastest, since foreign load can only slow an encode. Session telemetry gained the same tails (`enc_*`, `rtt_*`) and every session is retained as one JSON object per line for comparison. **Honest gaps, written into the plan:** decode/render percentiles were still open and phone-side at that commit (closed the same day by the client→server sample batch — see the five-tails bullet below), the "one desktop state, one link condition" claim is not true of a hosted runner, and the bench job's first outcome there will be `SKIP` until someone authors a baseline from its uploaded artifact. Gates: fmt, workspace clippy, `client`/`encode`/`wan` profiles, `cargo test --workspace` (~317) all green, plus the `#[ignore]`d real-encode bench test run explicitly.
- 2026-09-24: **Phase 1 step 5 (transport exposure) landed for the statistics that exist (a1590c3, 92e6465).** The step's framing — "surface what quinn and iroh already know" — is the whole scope, because path-MTU discovery and congestion control are library internals with no application surface: quinn already discovers the MTU and defaults to CUBIC behind a `TransportConfig` field, so the honest work is to *report* both, not implement either (the controller is deliberately left unswitched until a measurement says which tail it would move). `ConnectionStats` gained `congestion_events`/`cwnd_bytes`/`path_mtu`/`black_holes_detected` as `Option`, and the session recorder now emits a `LinkReport` per session: cumulative lost packets/bytes and datagrams/bytes moved, the **peak** congestion window (a window that collapsed and is still recovering must not read as uncongested), the number of times the selected path changed address (2155), and wall-clock seconds actually spent riding a relay (2157's "for how long"), with the relay interval closed at teardown so a session that ends still-relayed is billed to the end. **The asymmetry that was recorded here was wrong and is now corrected:** iroh's *connection-level* statistics do `Add<PathStats>` and discard the per-path values, but `Connection::paths()` → `Path::stats()`/`Path::rtt()` are public, so the WAN arm reads RTT and those four fields off the **selected** path (the one the session actually rides, which matters once DCUtR punches a direct path) and they appear on a WAN record exactly as they do on a LAN one; `None` — never `0` — now means only "this snapshot had no selected path". When this step first landed it asserted `path_stats()` was not re-exported and shipped those four fields as absent-on-WAN with a test locking that in; the claim was read off the aggregate, not off `Connection`. **`key=0` therefore always means "measured zero", in the tab-separated line and in JSON.** Corrected in the iroh-per-path commit; whether a live WAN session populates them is still owed as checklist item §"Transport exposure". Open with the reason written into the plan: per-path *split* (2154: iroh reports every open path and we sample only the selected one; quinn still flattens), per-channel backlog (2152), datagram queue state (2153), connection-ID rotation (2156), direct-vs-relay delta computed from the records (2158), failure attribution by layer (2159), live `stats --json` (2160), a schema version on the session record (2162), and any phone-side delivery (2170) — 2170 still needs the desktop→phone direction; the client→server half landed the same day with the five tails. A live loopback session (`server/tests/session_record.rs`, `#[ignore]`d) now drives `run_pipeline` on this host and asserts the link block fills in, so what remains device-gated is only that a real phone produces the `dec_*`/`rnd_*` samples the test injects — still owed as checklist item §"Transport exposure". Gates: fmt, workspace clippy, `client`/`encode`/`wan`/`client,wan` profiles, `cargo test --workspace` (core 211, server 83) and `--features wan` all green.
- 2026-09-24: **Docs truth pass: the generated 2000-item roadmap is replaced by a verified 3000-item backlog plus an execution plan, and README/AGENTS now describe the tree that exists.** Every one of `779608f`'s ids was audited against source (seven per-range passes plus first-hand verification), and the result is `docs/roadmap-3000.md`: ids 001-2050 keep their numbers with an explicit DONE / PARTIAL / WRONG verdict per range (default ABSENT), and 2051-3000 are 950 new items derived from what the audit surfaced — U live defects, V observability, W testing/CI/release, X security, Y transport + message classes, Z capture/compositor, AA input, AB media, AC files/clipboard/notifications, AD product surface, AE ecosystem. `docs/roadmap-execution-plan.md` is the order, the exit criteria and the gates, and its **Phase 0 is this synchronisation** — the plan's central rule is that a claim in a doc must name the artifact that measures it, which is how `frame_drops: 0`, "adaptive bitrate" and "desktop audio" all survived review. `docs/roadmap-2000.md` deleted (superseded docs go, they don't get archived). Headline verified facts now written down: **282 Rust tests / zero Kotlin tests** (`android/app` has no `src/test` at all), **zero** occurrences of percentile/p95/histogram in `core/src`, `server/src` or `android/app`, no `benches/`, CI is one ubuntu job whose `cargo audit || echo` cannot fail anything, and **`main` has been red on the last six pushes** — every one at `cargo fmt --all -- --check` (96 hunks / 22 files at `90d92df`). README's "Phase 6 Complete — Release Ready" badge became an honest beta badge; the five fake `linux-link --status/--rollback/--uninstall` verbs moved to `scripts/install.sh` where they live, the non-existent `--config` flag is gone, and the ten real verbs are listed. - 2026-09-24: **Green main (roadmap 2101/2102/2103), and the first honest measurement of the input-fairness claim.** `cargo fmt --all -- --check` passes, `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings` passes (also under `client`, `client,wan`, `wan`, `encode` core profiles), and `cargo test --workspace` is **279 passed / 0 failed / 13 ignored** — the first time all three have been true at once here, so `-D warnings` is now the gate rather than a baseline someone works around. Getting there: the documented "7-error clippy baseline" was a *build stop* in `capture.rs`/`capture_x11.rs`/`streamer.rs`, which is why nobody had ever seen the ~20 lints behind it in `server/`; the sweep also deleted two genuinely dead things (`PinEntry::phone_id`, `session_telemetry::MAX_LINE_BYTES`). `chaos_integration` no longer binds UDP 4716 (it fought the running user service for it) because `ChaosProxy::new` now binds, accepts port 0, and reports `local_addr()`; it also **counts every datagram as forwarded or dropped at decision time**, which replaced an assertion that inferred "chaos happened" from a 100-packet sample and therefore failed 2.65% of the time on its own RNG — the drop *rate* moved to a 100k-draw unit test where it is deterministic. **The finding: the project's stated input-fairness contract does not hold.** `video_flood_test` claimed to test it while connecting straight to the server and never through the chaos proxy it configured, so nothing was ever injected; routed properly at 10% datagram loss with a 1 MB/100 ms video flood, all 50 urgent packets do arrive, but their latency is **avg 2.2–3.8 s, max 6.3 s** against the "sub-300 ms" the test asserted (same flood at 0% loss: 12–89 ms). Quinn's `set_priority` does not bound the queue once retransmission pressure starts — nothing is lost, the urgent queue is simply seconds deep. The delivery half now asserts what is true, and the latency contract is a `#[ignore]`d test with these numbers in its reason so the gap stays measurable (`-- --ignored`); honest scope note: it drives **raw quinn streams**, not the production `streamer.rs` newest-frame/bounded-channel path, so a flood test against the real pipeline is still owed. Not done: the "green main" gate says *on a fresh clone and in CI* — commits are local, `main`'s CI is still red at the Format step until this is pushed, which is the user's call.
- 2026-09-24: **CI became a matrix (Phase 1 step 2) — written, not yet executed.** `.github/workflows/ci.yml` went from one job to four: `rust` (fmt, workspace clippy `--all-targets -D warnings`, release build, tests), `profiles` (a `fail-fast: false` matrix clippy-ing `core` under `client`, `client,wan`, `wan` and `encode`, and testing the last two), `android` (bridge clippy on host, then `cargo ndk -t arm64-v8a` to produce the gitignored `.so`, then `assembleDebug` + `lintDebug`), and `audit` with the `|| echo` removed so a dependency finding can actually stop a run. `release.yml` had two independent problems: the same `|| echo` pattern, and no apt dev packages — it links PipeWire/Wayland/libav*, so **its release binary has never been built by CI**, which is why `v0.1.0` is a tag with no artifact. The Android job's runner assumptions (NDK r29 discovered via `sdkmanager --list` rather than a rotting pinned string, Platform 37) are written from the repo's own build config and have **not** been observed on a runner: treat the first pushed run as a test of the workflow itself. Still unmet from that gate: *in CI*, which needs the push (the four-job matrix above has never executed and the pushed branch is red at `cargo fmt --check` until then) — the user's call, so "CI green" remains Phase 1 work. Phase 0 item 4 landed as **`core/src/capabilities.rs`: one generated source of truth** for negotiated protocol versions, transports, capture backends and codecs — every value is *read* from the constants and tables the wire uses (`capture_attempts`, `VideoCodec::ALL`, `AudioConfig::default()`, the ALPNs), feature-gated sections report *unavailable* rather than an empty list, and `linux-link capabilities` / `--json` / `--markdown` print it. `docs/capabilities.md` is that Markdown output, committed, and `server/tests/capabilities_doc.rs` fails while it is stale (verified by breaking it). Wire facts that had been duplicated across crates now exist once: `protocol::ALPN_V1_STREAM` (was four `"linux-link-stream"` literals), `v2::{V2_MIN_VERSION, V2_MAX_VERSION}` (both ends built their identity from their own `2`/`2`), and the mic relay's Opus rate/channels moved to `core::streaming::audio`. Also measured on the way and recorded rather than fixed: with the transport flood finally routed through the chaos proxy it configures, quinn stream priority gives **2.2-3.8 s average / 6.3 s max input latency at 10% loss** against the roadmap's sub-300 ms fairness contract, while delivering every packet — kept as an `#[ignore]`d test (ids 146-149 / 2449).
- 2026-09-24: **One shared v1 TCP control connection — the reconnect storm and the server's `Broken pipe` noise are the same bug, fixed in the bridge.** Root cause had nothing to do with the in-flight `core/src/protocol/*` work: every desktop *query* in `android/bridge/src/api.rs` opened its own throwaway connection to 1716/tcp, and the server's registry evicts by `device_id` (`state.rs::register_client`), so each `getBattery`/`get_monitors`/file-list evicted the live control registration and every *reply* left unread in the socket made the kernel send RST — which the plugins log as `Broken pipe (os error 32)`. The `StatsHud` 15 s battery poll is the ~4/min baseline, sheet/picker use the 18-36/min spikes, and the server's own `Kicked 1 stale session(s) due to reconnect storm` was firing on it. Now `connect_to_peer` pins the writer + peer address into `CONTROL_WRITER`/`CONTROL_PEER` and keeps a reader task feeding a broadcast (`INCOMING_PACKETS`); `control_request` subscribes **before** sending (the old `get_clipboard` subscribed after and could lose its own answer) and type-matches the reply, falling back to a dedicated connection only when there is no live session; the throwaway path half-closes (`shutdown()`) and drains to EOF so no RST is possible. Converted: clipboard, monitors, battery, privacy, audio, windows, exec, file-list, plus the four push-only calls (`send_clipboard`, `findmydevice`, `notification_reply`, `power`). Deliberately separate: `send_file`, because the desktop dials back our listener and the announcing socket must stay open. Bridge-only — no server, wire, or Kotlin change; reader teardown is guarded by `Arc::ptr_eq` identity and locks writer→peer in a fixed order so a dying session can neither clear nor dead-lock the replacement. Gates: bridge clippy `-D warnings`, bridge tests, core `client`-profile clippy, workspace clippy unchanged at the 7-error baseline, `cargo fmt`, arm64 `.so` rebuild, `assembleDebug` + `lintDebug` at the 18-finding baseline. **On-device confirmation still owed** (phone not attached): the journal must show no per-query `Incoming v1 TCP` and no `Broken pipe` WARN while sheets/pickers are in use. `cargo test --workspace` also throws `AddrInUse` in `chaos_integration::test_coffee_shop_wifi` — that test hardcodes UDP 4716, which the running user service owns; pre-existing in the in-flight test edits, not this change.

QUIC identity persistence (item 3 above) is what makes autostart usable: TOFU pins survive the service restart. **Tailscale path verified on-device**: phone (oppo-reno8-5g, <phone tailnet IP>) → laptop (<laptop tailnet IP>) with a full PIN pairing + streaming session + pixel-exact taps over the tunnel (UFW already allows 1716/tcp+udp, 4716/udp from anywhere); the iroh WAN path still needs a real cellular test (phone's cellular was OUT_OF_SERVICE).

Also `pair.rs` accepts `kdeconnect.identity` so PIN pairing can learn the phone deviceId, and the bridge shares the versioned `HANDSHAKE_HELLO/OK` constants (was sending the pre-versioning bare string). Tap-to-cursor mapping is pixel-exact through the letterbox; Esc via ShortcutBar lands.

- 2026-09-22: **Latency fix — encoder Auto-detect now runtime-verifies NVENC and the C2 ladder tries VAAPI before software** (`encoder_detect.rs` + `encoder.rs`). Root cause of the high-latency sessions on this hybrid box: `ffmpeg -encoders` lists NVENC at *compile* time, so `probe_encoders()` reported it available → Auto picked NVENC → sidecar died (driver 580.x < FFmpeg 9's API-13) → silent software-x264 fallback. `probe_encoders()` now confirms NVENC with a throwaway 1-frame `h264_nvenc` encode before listing it, and the sidecar-failure branch in `VideoEncoder::new` inserts a `open_vaapi_verified()` rung (in-process C4 → verified sidecar) before `degrade_to_software`, so a failed NVENC pick still lands on hardware. **Verified live over the tailnet**: server log "Auto-detect: selecting VAAPI → in-process VAAPI (h264_vaapi) on /dev/dri/renderD129", phone HUD steady at fps 26 / rtt 28 ms / e2e 55 ms (was rtt 47-70 ms / e2e 158 ms on the software rung). - 2026-09-21: R4 E4 landed — compositor privacy shield (**visual half; verified live on this Hyprland box**). `core/src/streaming/shield.rs` (under `capture`) paints a full-perimeter "session active" frame via `zwlr_layer_shell_v1` — a transparent-centre, opaque-red `PrivacyShield::show()` ring (`Layer::Overlay`, all-edge anchor, `KeyboardInteractivity::None` so it is click-through and never steals the remote's input), so bystanders see the machine is remote-driven while the desktop centre stays visible and usable. Binds `zwlr_layer_shell_v1`/`wl_shm` defensively → `None` no-op on GNOME/KDE/headless/X11 (same posture as B1). Geometry is a pure unit-tested fn (`render_shield`/`is_ring`/`shield_thickness`); an `#[ignore]`d live test maps the layer and asserts the edge pixels through the B1 screencopy backend — this is why E4's visual shield is box-verifiable, not device-gated (my earlier grouping of E4 with C4/E6 was wrong). Wired into Tier-3 #15 in `server/src/privacy.rs`: `GrabState.shield: Option<PrivacyShield>`, shown on the first successful `EVIOCGRAB` and dropped (unmapped) on `release_input`/TTL expiry, so the phone's **existing "Privacy: on/off" toggle** drives it end to end — **no wire packet, no new JNI (still 58), no Kotlin change**. Honest limits: input-block stays `EVIOCGRAB` (this Hyprland does NOT advertise `zwp_input_inhibitor_v1` → the compositor-native invisible-block is intentionally not attempted; the grab already blocks); the ring is text-free (no font rasteriser pulled in); frame geometry fixed at `show()` (mid-session resolution change is acked, not re-rendered). No new clippy lints after fixing two (`Closed` unit-variant pattern, test `chunks_exact`→`step_by`). Gates: `cargo test --workspace` (core 172, server 68) + `shield::` unit tests, `-p linux-link-core --features wan`, core `client`+`client,wan` profiles clean, workspace clippy at the unchanged 7-error baseline, capture-profile clippy clean on `shield.rs`, bridge clippy `-D warnings`, ndk arm64 bridge build, rustfmt; shield live test run explicitly and green (edge pixels confirmed painted — not a silent skip). Docs: roadmap E4 ✅ + §6 order, plan.md E4 bullet, checklist §9 shield checks.
- 2026-09-21: R4 E6 landed — foldable/tablet dual-pane (device behavior unverified — no foldable attached). `android/app/.../ui/FoldableLayout.kt`: `rememberFoldingSplit` observes `androidx.window` `WindowInfoTracker.windowLayoutInfo` → maps a `FoldingFeature` hinge to a split axis (vertical→left|right, horizontal→top|bottom, none→single); `TrackpadDock` is a standalone indirect-control surface (drag→`sendMouseEvent` relative, tap→`sendMouseClick` press+release, two-finger→wheel) on the same `limitedParallelism(1)` ordered dispatcher as the in-view trackpad; `ControlDock` = trackpad over the desktop `ShortcutBar`. `RemoteScreen`'s root became a `BoxWithConstraints`: the session (video + HUD + WAN/status + control buttons) sits in a **stream pane** sized via `Modifier.fillMaxWidth(0.5f)`/`fillMaxHeight(0.5f)`+`align`, dock in the other half; the pane `Box` is a stable subtree so a fold changes only its modifier — the `SurfaceView`/decoder is NOT torn down. Auto-engages on a hinge **or** ≥600 dp window (tablet/unfolded); a "Pane: auto/dual/single" button cycles the override; shortcut bar relocates stream→dock while dual. `androidx.window:window:1.5.0` added to app deps (already in Gradle cache → builds `--offline`). **No Rust/JNI change (58 exports).** en/es/ta strings (`pane_*`, `trackpad_hint`). Gates: `assembleDebug` + `lintDebug` clean offline — no new lint findings (only pre-existing `context.resources` + Int-state advisories on RemoteScreen). Honest limits: everything here is Kotlin layout over the existing input bridge; hinge geometry uses the stable `FoldingFeature.orientation` but real crease-gap inset/resizable-multi-window behavior is untestable without a foldable. Docs: roadmap E6 ✅ + §6 order, plan.md E6 bullet, checklist §17.
- 2026-09-21: R4 C4 landed — in-process VAAPI encoder, **live-verified on this box** (`core/src/streaming/encoder_vaapi.rs`): hand-rolled raw-FFI `av_hwdevice_ctx_create → AVHWFramesContext(NV12) → av_hwframe_get_buffer → av_hwframe_transfer_data → h264/hevc_vaapi` pipeline (ffmpeg-next 9.0 has no safe hwdevice bindings), BGRA→NV12 `sws_scale` upload, forced-IDR via `AVFrame.pict_type`, RAII `Drop`. `VideoEncoder` gained a `VaapiInProcess` variant routing `HardwareEncoder::Vaapi` in-process-first → verified sidecar → software (C2 ladder). Device selection was the whole game: **it tries each `/dev/dri/renderD*` and keeps the first that opens** (this hybrid box encodes on the Intel iHD `renderD129`, not the NVIDIA `renderD128` the old sidecar hardcoded), with a `LINUX_LINK_VAAPI_DEVICE` override; `build_ffmpeg_args` now derives the sidecar node the same way. NVENC in-process stays driver-gated (580.x < FFmpeg 9 NVENC API-13). Server-only `encode` feature; **no wire/JNI/Kotlin change (still 58 exports)**. Gates: `cargo test --workspace` (core 177, server 68) incl. `test_vaapi_encode_roundtrip` (real 40-frame `h264_vaapi`, self-skips where no VA node exists) + 4 pure ordering/value unit tests; `-p linux-link-core --features encode` clippy clean on the new files, core `client` + `client,wan` profiles clean, `cargo fmt` on touched files; workspace clippy unchanged at the pre-existing 7-error baseline (`capture.rs`/`capture_x11.rs`/`streamer.rs`, none in encoder files).
- Next: the entire R4 execution order is complete for everything verifiable on this box — A1–A3/E5, B1–B3, C1–C4, D1–D4, E1–E6 all landed (E4 compositor shield visual half verified live; E6 foldable dual-pane shipped device-unverified; C4 in-process VAAPI verified live). Only genuinely-hardware-gated residue remains: **NVENC in-process** (needs an NVIDIA driver ≥ the FFmpeg 9 NVENC API-13 baseline; this box is 580.x) and **E6 foldable/tablet on-device behavior** (needs a real foldable — the dual-pane code shipped compile/lint-clean). R2#3 is now fully done (software *and* VAAPI in-process). Everything else needs a real device the user tests directly (all Android items ship device-unverified).
